* **Offline maps: range up to 1000 km and detail level (all versions).** When downloading a map you choose a radius around the map centre (up to 1000 km) and a detail level; the app shows an estimated size and warns for a very large area.
* **Compare devices side by side (all versions).** In "MT_SW features" → "Connection center" → "Compare devices" a window with a card for each saved device: connected or not, battery and voltage, channel use and transmit time, uptime, active nodes and new messages. The readings history (trend of battery, channel use and node count) also builds up by itself in the background about every 10 minutes while the service runs, so the window need not be open. The data is the last reported, read from each device's own database; "Refresh" reads it again.
* **Remote contacts and lists in their own window (all versions).** Next to "On-demand diagnostics" there is a permanent entry opening a window with all favorites, ignored and contacts options of the remote device; it appears once the device was confirmed as yours and stays.
* **Dragging windows down on iPhone.** A window closes by dragging down from anywhere when its list is scrolled to the top.
* GPIO card (all versions): a wide "Get the list of pins" button asks the remote admin for the node's pin list (only that one settings section) and shows the pins to choose from; active only when the node is detected as MT_SW_FIRMWARE v3, greyed out otherwise.
* **A 3D view button on the map (all versions).** Above the zoom in and zoom out buttons there is a button that tilts the map into a 3D view; touching it again levels the map. The map can also be tilted with a two-finger gesture (on the computer, by dragging with the right mouse button). The map writes its tilt angle to the log, so it can be checked whether the gesture reaches the map.
* **Remote list management: pick from your own node list (all versions).** In the node details, in remote administration, you can pick from your own node list (with a search and multiple selection) the nodes to add to or remove from the favorites and ignored lists of the remote device, or to add as a contact (with the names from our list). A remote removal of a node from the remote device's node list was added too (with a confirmation). Viewing who is already on the remote lists needs a change in MT_SW_FIRMWARE and is not available yet.
* **Bridge: how long the copy of the configuration is kept and "Renew the copy now" (all versions).** The bridge keeps the copy of the configuration all the time and neither asks the radio nor disconnects the app; the time the copy stays valid is chosen in "Connection center", and the button renews it at once.
* **Unread badge on the app icon (iPhone/iPad and computer).** The number of unread messages is shown on the icon of the app (the iPhone home screen, the dock or taskbar of the computer where the system supports it); on Android the launcher draws the badge from the notifications itself.
* **Colour brightness (all versions).** In "Look" → "Advanced settings" a general brightness of the theme colours (five levels) and a separate brightness of the buttons ("As the general setting" or an own level). It does not change the main themes.
* **Console (all versions).** In "MT_SW features" → "Connection center" a screen with simple commands (help, clear, radios, radio, disconnect, remove, status, nodes, bridge), with hints while typing and the history of typed lines. All commands come from one list; disconnecting from the console does not make the radio icon pulse.
* **Background collection from the other radios (all versions, option in "Connection center", off by default).** Saved radios that are not in use are quietly connected, and the packets they hear go to their own databases (history, charts and statistics grow separately for every radio). The packet log is collected; the node list loads when the radio becomes the one in use.
* **Radio TCP bridge (all versions).** In "MT_SW features" → "Connection center" a local server that other programs connect to like to the radio (default ports 4404, 4405 and 4406 for radios 1–3). Off by default, separate settings for every radio: access from the local network, the port, the number of programs and restrictions (read only, blocking remote administration, blocking messages), all off by default. Answers to remote administration go back only to the program that asked.
* **Radio chooser in the top bar (all versions).** Up to three saved radios with a list in the top bar: the number of connected ones, switching by a tap, a swipe right connects or disconnects, a swipe left removes from the list. A soft pulse of the icon when a connected radio drops by itself (colour, speed and number in "Connection center"). The statistics of other nodes (telemetry, positions, neighbours, traceroutes, map reports) are synchronized between the databases of all radios (on by default: after a radio switch and every 15 minutes, newer data reaches every database), and the reception signal stays separate for each radio.
* **SOS widget: several taps instead of a hold.** A launcher starts editing the widget on a hold, so the alarm is now sent by several quick taps in a row (the number is chosen in the SOS screen: from 3, default 3; the widget shows "Tap N more"). On iPhone the last tap opens the app, which sends the alarm. The Android widgets have previews in the widget picker.
* **Activity status.** A small icon by a node in the list says whether someone is at the node (an app is connected to it): a green dot with a light outline means "active", a grey outline "inactive", and nothing is shown when the node does not share it; the tooltip says for how many minutes. A node shares its status once the switch in Settings → User is on (works from MT_SW_FIRMWARE v3; on an older one the switch is locked). "MT_SW features" has the options: showing, the icon look (dot, person with a dot, dot in a ring), replacing the favourite, muted or unmessageable icon, the colour, how long "active" holds (30 min to 12 h) and asking for the status when a chat is opened. The status query is also in "On-demand diagnostics" (and the firmware version moved to the very bottom). It works the same on Android, desktop and iPhone/iPad.
* **Activity in the node list, on the map and in the summaries.** New sorting "By activity (favourites first)": favourites first, then the active nodes, then the rest, each group by last heard. New "Active only" filter in the list and on the map (also in the HUD), and the header of the list shows the count of active nodes. "Network health" → summary has an "Activity" section (also in the report). The node details have the notification switches "when it becomes active" and "also when it becomes away"; the "Node activity" notification category is off by default (Android: the channel in the system settings; desktop and iPhone/iPad: the notification settings). Asking for the status is by hand only by default: "MT_SW features" has the buttons "Ask favourites / heard in the last hour / all for the status now" (one after another, with a pause, can be stopped) and the switch "Ask for the status by itself" (off by default, even when you have MT_SW_FIRMWARE v3 yourself). In "On-demand diagnostics" the status counts in the progress and in the report. All versions: Android, desktop, iPhone/iPad. The statistics widget (Android, iPhone/iPad, computer) and the permanent notification on Android show the number of active nodes next to the online ones, and the cards of favourites and the rest of the network show "Active now" or "Active X ago" (as a duration up to 7 days, then a date). The node menu in the list has "Ask for activity status". A node on firmware older than v3 is asked about its version again at most once a week. The "Network health" report has the history of activity changes of the last week.
* **SOS mode.** An "SOS" screen with a big button: an alarm with your position to the chosen channels (several can be chosen) and as a direct message to chosen nodes, also unmonitored ones. It is sent by holding the button, with repeating and confirmation. The message has the alarm bell, so for the receivers it arrives as an alarm.
* **SOS at hand.** A red "SOS" button with a yellow border next to the plate of your node in the top bar of every card (turn it off or choose the cards in the SOS screen), a tile in the Android quick settings, a "SOS MT_SW" shortcut (Siri, Shortcuts), an item in the tray icon menu on the computer, and an SOS widget on Android and iPhone/iPad (also the lock screen).
* **Neighbours on a map.** In the neighbours window of a node a new "Show on map" button: only the node and its neighbours with lines and the signal colour (SNR); when some have no position, a note says so.
* **Network coverage on the map.** The layer draws the approximate range of every node with a position (with the terrain), and a new "Propagation (weather forecast)" layer shows where the weather stretches the range; several areas can be added.
* **Node watchdog and availability.** A notification when a chosen node goes silent (with quiet hours and one shared notification for many nodes), and a chart of the availability history of a node.
* **Message archive.** An automatic copy of messages in a chosen folder, restoring from CSV, text and Markdown files, password encryption.
* **Range from measurements.** The route of a node coloured by signal strength, a comparison of two routes and a save of the range of the whole network to KML.
* **Terrain panorama.** Buildings as solids, forests, fog, light by the real sun, a panorama quality setting.
* **Link planner.** A comparison of results, antenna direction and tilt, antenna types, buildings and ground reflections on the map.
* **Statistics widget.** A new widget on Android, and on iPhone/iPad also small widgets for the lock screen; the plate of our node, a choice of which nodes to show.
* **iPhone and iPad like Android.** A QR code scanner, the event theme, import of KML/KMZ/GeoJSON layers, the terrain view with the camera, offline maps, background work, the full AI assistant, checks for new versions, the local TAK server, the Siri shortcut "MT_SW network status" and notification settings like on Android.
* **Computer.** The "Start with the computer" option and a single copy of the program by default.
* **Look.** "Saturated" and "Very saturated" themes, categories in the theme choice, a flat app icon option; the dark background is grey instead of bluish, and pure black in the "Saturated" and "Very saturated" themes (good for AMOLED screens).
* **Floating navigation bar.** The bottom tab bar (and on a computer or tablet the side bar too) is a rounded "island" with a name under each icon and the open tab highlighted; it looks the same on Android, the computer and iPhone/iPad, and a page's content ends above it, so the last item can always be scrolled into view. In "MT_SW features" the switch "Floating navigation bar at the bottom" brings the old bar back. Optionally (off by default) the bar hides while scrolling down (after a short, medium or long scroll) and returns on scrolling up or only at the very top of the page. The bar also has new icons (an outline, filled when open; the old ones can be brought back) and a vibration when the tab is changed.
* **Data, logs and diagnostics.** In "MT_SW features" a separate sub-screen with the logs (collecting, how long they are kept), the place of the app data, the message archive and the notification test; the main screen is shorter.
* **Look of the app.** A separate screen "Look" in "MT_SW features" (all versions) gathers the whole look in one place: theme, interface size, fonts, the app icon, the icon style (it applies to all icons of the app, in the settings too), the bar at the bottom and the touch. At the top are simple choices for areas (bars, cards, messages, fields, dialogs, map, assistant and network health) — plain, with depth, see-through or glass — plus "All at once" and ready-made looks (Classic, iPhone-like, Glass, Depth); below is a collapsible "Advanced settings" with a separate look, strength and blur for each of 12 groups. At the top is a live preview (a card, a bubble, a field and a bar) on a colourful background. Glass has depth: a sheen, a rim and a bright inner line. The top bar, the buttons and the toolbar on the map, the node details, the settings cards, the assistant, network health and the log lists follow the chosen look too. Bottom bar: full tab names (they shrink to fit), a see-through selection pill, add buttons above the bar; the Messages, Nodes, Settings and Network health lists scroll under it. The strong vibration while the send button is held lasts until you let go (the system's native vibration). By default the app starts with the glass look and the iPhone-style icons (change it in "Look", for example with the ready-made "Classic" look). It uses the Haze library (Chris Banes, Apache-2.0). The window with the neighbour details has the buttons "Show on map" and "Close". "Try notifications" has three buttons in "Data and logs". "What's new" shows the whole history from the beginning. The theme picker marks the theme in use (colour and a tick), and its name is shown under the "Theme" line; each theme in the list has a small circle with its background and accent colour. The cards and buttons of the whole app (also on the Connection screen and in the connection-type switch) follow the chosen look — glass or depth; glass where blurring is impossible (the map) is denser, and the icons on the map toolbar get a colour matched to the glass, so they are always visible. The log and metric lists run under the floating bar (no full strip at the bottom), and dialogs are never more see-through than 15% (set it with the "Window transparency" slider on the "Look" screen). On the iPhone a tab change is accompanied by a Taptic Engine tap, and holding the SOS button and the send button vibrates until released. Less work while scrolling: the look options are read once from the top of the app, redrawn icons are remembered, and the content is recorded for blurring only when the bar uses glass.
* **Channel description.** Your own note of up to 200 characters on a channel (in the channel editor), kept on the phone only. When a description is set, the conversation list shows it in place of the last message; without one the last message is shown (no separate switch).
* **Data.** "Add data from a backup" and synchronisation of the device statistics.
* **MT_SW_FIRMWARE v3.** A query for LAN network statistics in On-demand diagnostics and reading of the phone's Bluetooth signal strength.
* **Alarm when an SOS is received.** An SOS from another copy of the app (it has a hidden mark, so an ordinary text with the word SOS starts nothing) opens the conversation, covers the whole screen with a big SOS and rings again and again until you confirm it. On Android and iPhone/iPad also on a locked phone (a siren in a loop, vibration, the torch). The alarm window has ready answers to the sender, "Show on the map" and "Open the conversation", and confirming lets the sender know the SOS was seen. The "Test the alarm" button in the SOS screen tries it on this device.
* **SOS: chosen nodes, quiet hours, history.** The full alarm can be limited to chosen nodes and quiet hours can be set. Received SOS are saved in the history with "Open the conversation" and "Show on the map" buttons. The "Send SOS again" button repeats the alarm when nobody confirmed. The quiet hours have a list of exceptions of their own, for each node in the list you can choose the sound (siren, bell, silent), the history can be saved to a file, and repeating can be set to "No limit". Optionally the alarm goes off only after a second SOS from the same node. On the sender's side the "Who confirmed the SOS" button shows the list of confirmations. A channel or node can have a separate SOS text, and the history cleans itself after the chosen number of days. When someone answers your SOS, the repeating stops, you get a notification, and the list of confirmations can be saved to a file. "Calling for help" has the bell sound, the SOS notification has answer buttons, and on the map the nodes that answered have a ring.

##### Improvements

* Map (all versions, most of all iPhone and iPad): smoother panning, more accurate taps on node bubbles, a "pick a node" list for nodes in the same place, the "Max. hops" and "Max. distance from my node" filters; the + and − buttons are bigger and no longer overlap the map credits, and the icon bars are more transparent.
* Faster node statistics: every screen reads the full history only of the data it shows; the newest data appears at once and the full history follows right after (most of all on iPhone and iPad).
* Terrain panorama: smoother ridges, new mountain and forest textures, faster drawing.
* Charts can skip readings with a wrong node clock (option, off by default); the database writes faster and the logs on iPhone/iPad are much faster.
* The bottom tabs remember their screen (tapping an open tab again returns to its first screen), and tapping a notification opens the conversation it came from (also on iPhone, iPad and the computer).
* "Last heard" in plain language, relative up to 7 days ("yesterday", "the day before yesterday"), older as a date.
* Logs (all versions): the 24-hour log is always on, is saved in a separate file in the chosen folder, the full log keeps up to seven days of recorded time, and every bug report always includes all logs. Logs on iPhone/iPad work like on Android (switches, parts, crash records, a folder in Files).
* Bluetooth: the log has one packet summary per minute instead of a separate line for every packet.
* Remote administration also asks the remote node for its MT_SW_FIRMWARE version and local statistics.
* Route history: "Requested manually" and "Received automatically" labels; traceroute replies to other nodes (sniffer mode) are also saved as observed.
* The data consent window scrolls, names the logs as the only data for the developer and asks again when its text changes; the consent window for chat photos says where the photo goes.

##### Fixed bugs

* iPhone/iPad: shifted taps on node bubbles on the map; no notification sound on a locked screen and in the background; the backup error (saving and reading files from Files); the screen turning off during an update; the Bluetooth link to the radio not returning after a break; the Bluetooth update not finding the device in update mode; "Neighbour info" closing the app.
* Remote GPIO (all versions): writing a pin waits for the node's confirmation and shows the result; "Read" always ended with no answer because the app did not accept the module's reply.
* Interface scaling (all versions): dialogs, list windows, drop-down menus, the date and device pickers, the photo window and tooltips opened in a separate system window that lost the set scale and showed big letters.
* Network health and node details: the current and voltage line in the environment statistics is continuous again (it was cut into pieces); the search field no longer jumps the cursor after every letter; series names are in Polish and noise has the unit dBm.
* Node neighbours: the report list no longer crashes the app on repeated entries, and the window has one "Close" button instead of two identical ones.
* Computer: the back arrow on the node details card closes the open statistics at once and shows the list.

##### Changes against the original

* The original's “Telemetry” and “Node Tracker” widgets are removed from the code as well (the telemetry widget code came back with the migration): only our own widgets remain, MT_SW statistics and SOS.
* Merged changes of the original (version 2.8.5 and later): among others the radio setup wizard, QR/NFC connection codes, an nRF52 bootloader update over Bluetooth and newer libraries.
* The "Local statistics" and "Node tracker" widgets were removed: one MT_SW widget is left.
* iPhone/iPad: the oldest supported system is iOS 17.5, and the app identifier is pl.sw.mt, like on Android.
* The build was adapted to the new Gradle.
* Environment metrics: all values (temperature, humidity, pressure and others) are on one shared chart, like before; the original split them into separate charts. Power stays in its own tab.
* Light themes: the white is always slightly grey (unlike the original), without a separate switch.
* Floating bar: no edge line by default (a separate "Bar edge line" setting), slightly darker on the map card, and "Network health" has its name on two lines.
* Removed AI models whose licences do not fit GPL-3.0 (the NLLB-200 translator, TranslateGemma, Llama-based DeepSeek models, Llama 3.2 1B); translation uses freely licensed models. Assistant answers carry an AI label and translations an (AI) note.
* Added the NOTICE and PRIVACY.md files (PL and EN); the "google" variant is built the same way as F-Droid, without Google services.

#### From the beginning of the app

##### New features

* **Network health.** Tabs for power, signal, airtime, environment, traffic, neighbours and resources (CPU, memory, flash), a "Network condition" card, a summary with rankings, charts in node details and export to PDF, HTML, Markdown, text and CSV; the data is decoded live from the mesh log.
* **AI assistant, translator and dictation offline.** One engine (llama.cpp) and one choice of models (Gemma, Qwen, Bielik, DeepSeek), using the NPU and the graphics card. The assistant calculates by itself, knows the app and the network, remembers the conversation, can read aloud and, if you want, use the internet; the translator works without a network and dictation turns speech into text in the chat.
* **Link planner (Mesh Link Planer) and antenna calculator.** A native planner with terrain, obstacles, live weather and the coverage on the map, a cable and connector wizard and the dimensions of 13 kinds of antennas.
* **Network view (terrain panorama).** The silhouette of the terrain all around with the nodes, turned by the compass, offline terrain and a camera view (AR).
* **On-demand diagnostics and sniffer.** Ten kinds of queries to nodes (port 354) and a radio and MQTT sniffer on one screen: grouping of duplicates with a list of receivers, saving and loading the log (txt, JSON, CSV), coloured packet categories, decoding on tap and a buffer of 5000 packets.
* **Device logs and signal quality.** Live device logs with search and export, LNA gain correction for noise and RSSI, an SNR rating that depends on the preset and status colours that agree across the whole app.
* **Map.** A shared MapLibre engine on Android and the computer, the OpenStreetMap map by default, terrain shading, a weather radar, offline maps, spreading nodes with the same position, better clustering and a view fitted to all the nodes.
* **Nodes.** The name of the relaying node and the full list of relays in the delivery status, adding a contact by ID, remote favourite and ignore over the LoRa network, remote GPIO control, clearing the node database (also automatic and over Bluetooth), a node colour when generating a key, extra MT_SW_FIRMWARE telemetry.
* **Routes and neighbours.** Traceroute on the map with signal strength, signal quality lines between nodes, a neighbours icon in the node list and passive NeighborInfo.
* **Chat.** Long messages split and put together on the fly, photos by link and a preview of images from links, translation before sending, reading aloud, quick command buttons (/ping, /hello, /test) and Quick Chat templates.
* **MT_SW features and documentation.** A separate screen with the features of the fork, a knowledge base, all the documentation in the app in Polish and English, a "What MT_SW adds" welcome screen and the restored Traffic Management screen.
* **Themes and look.** Black-and-white themes, "App scaling", any node colour, an own icon and colour palette and an "About" screen.
* **Notifications.** Adaptive notifications (Android 16+) and notifications about new versions of the app.
* **App for the computer and iPhone/iPad.** Versions for macOS, Windows and Linux with a welcome screen, a quick scroll bar and a third column that follows the selected node, and the base of the iPhone/iPad app.
* **Support.** "Support the project", reporting bugs and ideas, collecting logs.

##### Improvements

* A smoother app: lighter node and chat lists, the map, node details and Network health with a lot of data, a faster start on the computer.
* A full Polish translation (over a thousand texts), modem preset names in Polish with the English name in brackets, Polish device role names.
* The same colours across the whole app: gold = good, red = fair, purple = bad; trust in a node in gold, a key mismatch in red.
* Noise is rated after the LNA correction, the SNR rating depends on the preset.
* LoRa settings: frequency offset, coding rate, the Narrow Fast preset and 869.44165 MHz by default; warnings about the narrow band and about leaving the region.
* Offline maps also for the default OpenStreetMap map and smaller badges with a tail.
* "i" descriptions next to features, calmer information windows, a scroll bar on the computer; on the computer Enter is a new line and Ctrl+Enter sends the message.

##### Fixed bugs

* **Top bar with several columns (computer, tablet).** The node plate and the radio chooser take at most a part of the bar, so with two columns the title and the buttons of the screen are not pushed out of the window.
* Reconnecting Bluetooth to an already paired device (Xiaomi/MIUI).
* Empty drop-down lists and a map that did not work in the packaged computer version.
* Recovering a device locked in DFU mode and updating with an own file for unknown devices.
* The app closing when using the translator and with a huge amount of data in Network health.
* Clearing nodes over Bluetooth now works to the end; sending photos by link and losing diagnostics queries fixed.
* Computer: double screens in the navigation and wrong columns after a saved session.
* Correct hop counts in the route history and "Route time" instead of English text; the position trail map no longer starts in the ocean.

##### Changes against the original

* Renamed to MT_SW_APP with an own icon and an own identifier, so the app installs next to the original.
* A native link planner instead of the external Site Planner.
* The NLLB-200 translator model removed (its licence does not agree with ours).
* Obsolete device roles hidden (REPEATER, ROUTER_CLIENT); "Heap" is now called RAM.
* Further merges with the original (protobufs, libraries, translations, new features), with our Polish translations taking priority.

## Full change log by version

Every new change goes under the heading of the current version.

### v6 (2.8.6) — test versions from 28 November 2026

* **Labels on a Niimbot D110 printer (Android, desktop, iPhone/iPad).** “Print label” (QR code) when sharing a contact or channels and on the connected device card. Android only in the original. Not yet tried with a printer on desktop and iPhone.
* **Transparent mode warning.** When the device only passes raw frames to another program, the app warns, holds sending and can turn the mode off.
* **Red open lock in a conversation.** A reply encrypted with a channel key stays in the same direct chat and is marked.
* **Bluetooth: newer Kable library 0.46.0 (all versions).** When connecting on Android fails while setting the packet size, the next attempt skips that request. Desktop reads the packet size from the device. Still to be checked on devices.
* **Newer AI engines (all versions).** The assistant, translator and dictation use llama.cpp b12774 and whisper.cpp 1.9.6. The app downloads the engine again. Wire (OnDemand and Sniffer) updated to 7.2.2.
* Updated to the original app, version 2.8.6.

### v5 (2.8.5) — test versions from 5 November 2026

* Desktop and iPhone/iPad: previously connected Bluetooth devices now show on the Connect list right away, without scanning — the way Android already does with system pairing.
* Remote lists (all versions): the window remembers the nodes last picked separately for favorites, ignored nodes and added contacts; chips to tap, "Use all" and "Clear this list" appear under the picker (each memory is cleared on its own).
* Windows (all versions): dragging down now also closes windows with a scrolling list (e.g. map layers, map filter, planner); before, a move taken by the list was not counted and only windows without scrolling closed.
* Windows (all versions): a window opened from inside another window (node picker for remote lists, a hike from the hikes list) has its own solid background, so the text of the window below no longer shows through, and light/dark colours inside windows are judged from the theme background (not from the window's transparent surface).
* Interface scale in windows (all versions): dialogs, list windows, drop-down menus (also the selection lists in settings), the date and time picker, the device chooser and the photo window open in a window of the system of their own that lost the scale set in the app (interface and text size, e.g. 70%) and showed the default large letters. Now each such window takes the scale from the place that opens it, so it also holds in the hikes window.
* Hikes (all versions): the route charts (height, climb, speed, pace, distance, signal) are in the style of the node statistics charts: a glass card, a gradient under the line, glass drops on the measurements, a value axis on the left and a time axis below. All charts share one time axis (from the start to the end of the hike). A touch or a drag shows the value and the time at that place. The chart style follows the "Chart style in node details" setting in "Look". When the times of the signal readings lie outside the route, the signal chart has its own time axis.
* Hikes (all versions): the button "Save hike to a file (JSON)" saves the whole hike (route, places and picture links where they were uploaded) to a place chosen on the device, without sending anything to a server.
* Signal survey (all versions): the card of a tapped square shows the best reading as before, and below it an expandable list "All readings in this square": node, RSSI, SNR, time and whether the reading was direct or via a relay (the best one has a star). Readings from one place no longer merge into a single point.
* Hikes (all versions): touching or dragging on one chart shows the same moment on all the route charts (height, speed, distance, signal).
* Hikes (all versions): the hikes list has a "Load a hike from a file (JSON)" button that loads a hike saved earlier to a file (the same format as sharing). Read errors go to the log only.
* Hikes (all versions): a hike can be saved with its photos to one ZIP archive ("Save hike with photos to an archive (ZIP)" in the hike details), and the same load-from-file button takes JSON or ZIP. The archive is read without the internet. Read errors go to the log only.
* Interface scale (all versions): tooltips (the bubbles next to icons) also keep the app's chosen scale.
* Hikes (all versions): one shared clock for the whole hike: route points from the phone get the same time (the phone's clock) as the signal readings and the device's points, so the route and signal charts share one time axis.
* Remote GPIO (all versions): new icons with a magnifier: a 0/1 waveform for reading a pin's state (instead of the rounded arrows) and a chip with pins for loading the list of pins. Drawn as solid shapes, so the "outline" style shows them as a hollow contour.
* iPhone: the camera for messages has its own buttons (shoot, flip, cancel), so it can be left at any moment; the photo goes straight to the question whether to send it.
* Picture folder (phone and tablet): settings show the MT_SW album, the room taken by the pictures and the free storage, like on the computer.
* GPIO card (all versions): the "Get the pin list" button has its own pin-header icon instead of the refresh circle (the read button has the same circle).
* Remote contacts, favorites and ignoring (all versions): the original (Meshtastic-Android, commit 3147011b87, #8251, 25 November 2026) added its own "Contacts and favorites" screen for a remote node. Our panel of remote lists in the node details stays; it does the same and additionally lets you pick many nodes at once from your own list and type an ID by hand; the screen from the original is not included. Three solutions were taken from the original: a node from our database is sent to the remote device with its public key (a node typed in by its ID goes without a key), on a device with the Client Base role the favorite is set only after the contact is confirmed, and the app asks for confirmation before adding to favorites and before "Ignore" on a remote device. An exported contact (for example in a QR code) always carries a correct key (a fix from the original).
* Pop-up windows instead of sliding lists (all versions): every such window (for example the map layers, the message menu) has a close cross at the top and also closes with a swipe down from anywhere on the window (with a scrolling list only when the list is at the very top, so normal scrolling never closes the window); the planner keeps its own original cross, without a second one.
* Logs (all versions): the log of the last 24 hours is always on at the detailed level and cannot be switched off (a note replaced the switch); the full log is still written when switched on and is cleaned after at most seven days of recorded time. The data consent has a new point about logs and appears again so that everyone accepts it.
* Logs (all versions): on first start and until a folder for the logs is chosen, the app shows a folder choice screen (after the data consent). The log zips are saved in the chosen folder; the folder can be changed in the settings.
* Bug report (all versions): every report always carries all the logs (the last 24 hours, the full log up to seven days and the crash records), with no switch.
* Logs (all versions): the log now holds the screen changes (type names of screens only) and the settings in effect at start and at every change (values of switches only), without personal data.
* Remembered devices (all versions): the smallest number of remembered device databases is 3.
* Device chooser (all versions): the plate of a device always shows the real short name (never the end of the node number), appears at once after connecting and takes its data from the same source as the "Connect" card; fixed for a node that changed its number and for addresses stored in another form. The reasons why a plate could not be drawn go to the log.
* Node neighbours (all versions): the list of neighbour reports no longer crashes the app on repeated entries (a duplicate list key).
* Device chooser (all versions): the plate of every device now always comes from the database of that device. After switching the displayed device, the other one no longer shows a plain name instead of its plate: the plate is read as soon as the device is shown and when it connects, a node of another device is never taken for it, and the last known plate is kept meanwhile.
* Our look in menus, sheets and cards (all versions): the pop-up menus (node list filter and sort, the node menu on right click or long press, the device chooser and others), bottom sheets and cards that used to have the plain look now have our style: rounded corners, a shadow (a light glow on a dark theme), a thin rim and a sheen. Menus and sheets follow the look of the "windows" group, and cards the look of their group of cards in "Look" (plain, depth, see-through, glass), so everyone can adjust them. The device chooser list now has a visible shadow on a dark theme too.
* Message delivery status window (all versions): the nodes that relayed the message are shown as plates with their short name and colour (below the relay count, wrapping to the width), instead of the full name and the byte number. The duplicated relay information (the count and "Relayed by...") is merged into one. This also applies to the reaction status window.
* Pop-up windows (all versions): every window now has our style — a shadow like the cards, a thin rim and a gradient sheen (also windows that used to be plain and flat because they used a separate standard window).
* Node list shadow (all versions): by default the card shadow now stays still (it used to blink on every packet because it followed the "last heard" time). The option "Blinking shadow of nodes" now works on the list too (colours are fixed, no choice): the card of your radio blinks together with the connection icon (blue when sending, green when receiving), and the cards of other nodes blink in the theme colour when a packet arrives from them.
* Node list (desktop): the right mouse button on a node opens the same menu as a long press (e.g. send a route), so the button no longer has to be held down. The left button still opens the details.
* Connection icon (all versions): the icon of the connected radio is now a handheld radio with an antenna and a wave (instead of the router look). It follows the icon style from the Look settings, so by default it matches the other icons, and the Standard style shows it in the original filled form. Colours and blinking stay as before.
* Antenna radiation plot (antenna calculator and planner, all versions): the inside of the outline has a glass gradient, stronger towards the main direction, with a pale sheen at the top and a thin light line along the outline.
* Terrain panorama (all versions): the terrain has a glass gradient with a light outline along the ridge; the rock and forest texture is off by default and is turned on in the "Options" with the "Rock and forest texture" setting.
* Terrain panorama (all versions): the buildings are glass too: each wall has a gradient from a lighter top to a darker foot, a thin light outline and a soft sheen over the roof. The node chip in the list of direct messages is the same glass chip as everywhere else.
* Terrain panorama (all versions): every node in view has its chip with the short name, not only the 40 nearest (the rest were plain dots).
* Node chips (all versions): a glass look with a light gradient (lighter top), a thin outline and a soft shadow — the same in the lists, the top bar, on the map and in the terrain panorama. On the map the chip's tail is a separate outlined triangle with a small gap below the chip, and below it a dot at the node's exact spot.
* Map (all versions): the lines between nodes have a thin light outline on both sides of the line's colour.
* Terrain panorama (all versions): the choice buttons (range, nodes up to, antenna, compass) are glass, with a gradient, an outline and the shadow of the chosen look; the chosen one has a yellow outline. The node chips in the panorama look like those on the map, and the colour of the outline still tells whether the node stands above the ridge.
* Network health (all versions): node cards cast their shadow again (the rounding only clipped their inside, not the whole card).
* Percent signs (all versions): "Node availability", the console command "battery" and a few descriptions showed "72%%" instead of "72%".
* Console (all versions): the command suggestion buttons have their own fill and the shadow no longer shows through them (on Windows they looked like a grey smear); on a dark theme they have a thin rim so the upper edge is visible.
* Building on GitHub: the files of each system go into the archive as bare files in one folder, without separate sub-folders (msi, exe, jars etc.).
* iPhone/iPad, Release build: one link optimisation phase (removing redundant file-initialiser calls) is switched off; it took about 34 of 52 minutes. The rest of the optimisation stays on.
* Console (all versions): the "TCP bridge" line of the "status" command shows a short state ("not available" with the reason) instead of a sentence asking to save a device; the new command "position <latitude> <longitude> [altitude]" sets a fixed position on the connected device, like --setlat, --setlon and --setalt of the Meshtastic command line. The rest of the Meshtastic command-line commands were added too: "info", "send", "reboot", "shutdown", "factory-reset", "reset-nodes", "remove-node", "favorite", "ignored", "traceroute", "request-position", "telemetry", "owner", "time", "ringtone", "canned" and "gpio" (reboot, shutdown, factory reset and node reset need the word "confirm"). In place of --set, --get, --ch-set, --export-config and --configure: "set", "get" (over 60 settings named like in Meshtastic, e.g. lora.region), "export", "channels", "channel-set" and "ham". Plus our own commands: "connect", "messages", "signal", "nearby", "battery", "loop", "search" and "notify".
* Connecting a second device (all versions): while one device is shown and connected, a device picked on the Connect tab is only saved and connected in the background (when background collection is on); the full-screen screen and the change of the shown data happen only when you switch the device in the top bar.
* AI assistant (all versions): the shadow of the suggestions sits right against the buttons, because the invisible touch box that pushed it away from the button is gone.
* Network health (all versions): the node cards have the same side margins as the "Network condition" card and the search bar, so they are no longer narrower than the rest of the screen.
* Menu button above the bottom bar, e.g. adding a contact (all versions): it has the same glass look as the other buttons and follows the style chosen in "Look".
* Switched-on switches in the settings (all versions): a row with a switched-on switch (in "MT_SW features" too) no longer has the flat blue-grey background, but a light glass tint in the colour of the theme (lighter at the top) and a thin yellow outline all around, each such row being its own glass pane; fitting both the dark and the light theme.
* Building on GitHub: all the built files (Android, Windows, Linux, macOS, iPhone/iPad) go into one common download, "MT_SW_APP-wszystko", instead of a separate folder for each system.
* iPhone/iPad: start-up fix — the app did not start after installing, because the iOS module lacked the database settings store.
* Badge with a number on the app icon (iPhone/iPad, computer): it shows all unread messages, also from devices kept in the background; on iPhone it is set outside the screen, so it also changes while the app is in the background. On Android the launcher builds the number from the notifications.
* Floating navigation bar (all versions): the glass of the bar no longer has a darker band in its lower part, which looked like an odd white strip around the names.
* "Connect" window (all versions): the buttons of a device card share the width of the card and are never wider than it, a long name ends with dots (on a computer and iPad they no longer push the columns out of the window).
* Statistics sync between devices (all versions): it only runs when both devices are on the same radio settings (region, preset, frequency); it copies only the nodes that are on the lists of both devices, mainly telemetry and positions, and the signal strength (RSSI) and SNR are never carried over — each device keeps its own reception. A device kept in the background remembers its radio settings in its own database so that this check works for it too.
* Background devices and the TCP bridge (all versions): a device kept in the background has its own database at once (the plate with the short name, the node list, messages and packets are no longer lost), the bridge answers clients like a real radio (the first stage is the configuration only, the second stage the nodes only — switching the device through the bridge no longer hangs), the TCP connection retries at most every 15 s, after three failed tries the app retries by itself every 30 s (no error that needs choosing the node again), background devices send a keep-alive every 30 s, the console gives the real reason when there is no bridge state, and a waiting screen with a big loading circle covers the app while another device is being shown. On iPhone too precise time values on charts no longer close the app. The log lines "admin answer without a request" and a short loss of position in the background are ordinary entries, not warnings.
* Plate of your node in the top bar (all versions): on the "Network health" and AI assistant cards (and in Node details when another node is shown) tapping the plate opens the details of your node, like on the other cards.
* Search bar on the node list and in "Network health" (all versions): it is a clear glass pane with a thin outline and a soft gradient, with no solid fill; it is grey while empty, and once you type something and the list is filtered, the outline and the gradient smoothly turn the yellowish colour of the theme, like the buttons.
* Connection card (all versions): under the device name there is a list of the steps of this connection attempt (finding, pairing, connecting, checking, loading nodes); finished steps carry a tick, the one in progress a small spinner, and the list starts again with each new attempt. A network connection shows its own, shorter list.
* iPhone/iPad: the phone's GPS rests while the phone stands still (like on Android): once several fixes in a row stay inside the cell in which the radio judges stillness, the phone stops asking for its position and saves battery; it resumes sending when the accelerometer detects movement or the cell changes. The radio keeps broadcasting the last position it was given, so the network loses nothing.
* "Outline and glass" buttons (all versions, the default): buttons, floating buttons and coloured icon buttons are a thin outline on a clear glass pane; a working one has a soft yellowish glow in the colour of the theme, a switched-off one is grey. On the "Look" screen the "Buttons" list also offers "Filled glass" and "Classic" (back to the old look), and the advanced settings have the strength of the glow and the thickness of the outline; the gradient (3D feeling) works with every style.
* Progress bars and sliders (all versions): the empty track is black on the light theme and light grey on the dark theme, and the filled part changes from grey to the colour of the theme (yellow in the default one) as the progress grows; the same for switches and check boxes.
* Backups (all versions): "App data backup" now saves everything in one file — the radio's data and all the settings; the new "App settings backup" saves the settings alone; the "Look" screen has "Save the look to a file" and "Restore the look from a file" (a restore returns the look to the defaults, then loads the saved one). The files are the same on Android, computer and iPhone/iPad, so they can be moved between systems; older backups (a plain database file) still load.
* Signal survey (all versions): the "Record" switch with the session history, the heatmap and the CSV export is now in the map layers sheet on iPhone/iPad too (with the phone's location or the radio's GPS) and on desktop (with the GPS position of the connected radio; while the radio has not reported one yet, the line under the switch says so).
* Channel description (all versions): the channel editor has a field "Channel description (only in this app)" for a note of up to 200 characters, kept on the phone only and tied to the channel (name and key), not to its place. When a description is set, the conversation list shows it in place of the last message; without one the last message is shown (no separate switch).
* Floating navigation bar (all versions: Android, computer, iPhone/iPad): the bottom tab bar (and on a computer or tablet the side bar too) is a rounded "island" with a name under each icon and the open tab highlighted, the same on every platform. A page's content ends above the bar, so the last item can always be scrolled into view; while the keyboard is open the bar hides. In "MT_SW features" the switch "Floating navigation bar at the bottom" (on by default) brings the old bar back, and the optional, off-by-default "Hiding the bar while scrolling down" (after a short, medium or long scroll) shows it again after a short or longer scroll up or only at the very top of the page. The new bar icons (an outline, filled when open) can be turned off ("New bar icons"), and there is a vibration when the tab is changed (also optional).
* Data, logs and diagnostics (all versions): the logs (collecting and retention), the place of the app data, the message archive and the notification test moved from the main "MT_SW features" screen to a separate sub-screen; the settings search and the data-logs link lead to it.
* **Look of the app.** A separate screen "Look" in "MT_SW features" (all versions) gathers the whole look in one place: theme, interface size, fonts, the app icon, the icon style (it applies to all icons of the app, in the settings too), the bar at the bottom and the touch. At the top are simple choices for areas (bars, cards, messages, fields, dialogs, map, assistant and network health) — plain, with depth, see-through or glass — plus "All at once" and ready-made looks (Classic, iPhone-like, Glass, Depth); below is a collapsible "Advanced settings" with a separate look, strength and blur for each of 12 groups. At the top is a live preview (a card, a bubble, a field and a bar) on a colourful background. Glass has depth: a sheen, a rim and a bright inner line. The top bar, the buttons and the toolbar on the map, the node details, the settings cards, the assistant, network health and the log lists follow the chosen look too. Bottom bar: full tab names (they shrink to fit), a see-through selection pill, add buttons above the bar; the Messages, Nodes, Settings and Network health lists scroll under it. The strong vibration while the send button is held lasts until you let go (the system's native vibration). By default the app starts with the glass look and the iPhone-style icons (change it in "Look", for example with the ready-made "Classic" look). It uses the Haze library (Chris Banes, Apache-2.0). The window with the neighbour details has the buttons "Show on map" and "Close". "Try notifications" has three buttons in "Data and logs". "What's new" shows the whole history from the beginning. The theme picker marks the theme in use (colour and a tick), and its name is shown under the "Theme" line; each theme in the list has a small circle with its background and accent colour. The cards and buttons of the whole app (also on the Connection screen and in the connection-type switch) follow the chosen look — glass or depth; glass where blurring is impossible (the map) is denser, and the icons on the map toolbar get a colour matched to the glass, so they are always visible. The log and metric lists run under the floating bar (no full strip at the bottom), and dialogs are never more see-through than 15% (set it with the "Window transparency" slider on the "Look" screen). On the iPhone a tab change is accompanied by a Taptic Engine tap, and holding the SOS button and the send button vibrates until released. Less work while scrolling: the look options are read once from the top of the app, redrawn icons are remembered, and the content is recorded for blurring only when the bar uses glass.
* What's new in the app (all versions): the "from the beginning" view no longer has a version number in its title, the "from the beginning of the app" section has much more content and is tied to no version, and "Since the last stable" keeps the range shown after an update.
* Merge with the original (15 Nov): newer protobufs (2.8.2.31), refreshed hardware, firmware and translation data and the ANZ region name with Brazil. The change from the original that limited the location precision on some channels was not taken over: here everyone chooses the precision themselves. Nothing changes in our features.
* Alarm when an SOS is received (all versions): an SOS sent by another copy of MT_SW_APP carries a hidden, invisible mark in its text (an ordinary text with the word SOS has none, so it starts nothing). A received one opens the conversation, covers the whole screen with a big SOS with the sender's name and the text, and rings until you confirm it. Android: an alarm notification that rings again and again (alarm sound, vibration, opens over the lock screen, asks to break through Do Not Disturb); computer: the window comes back from the tray and a siren plays in a loop; iPhone/iPad: a siren in a loop on the playback channel (it sounds on a locked phone and with the silent switch on, also when "Background work" keeps the app alive), vibration, and when the app is not running a new notification with the same siren every 10 s for 10 minutes (removed on confirming); phones (Android and iPhone/iPad) also flash the torch. In the alarm window: two ready answers to the sender ("I am coming to you", "I am calling for help" — a direct message), "Show on the map" (the map centred on the sender) and "Open the conversation"; plain "Confirm" also sends the sender a short "SOS seen, I am reacting". In the SOS screen: the "Alarm when an SOS is received", "Flash the torch in the alarm" and "Tell the sender when I confirm" switches and the "Test the alarm (only on this device)" button. The mark protects against accidents, it is not a lock: the app's code is open.
* SOS, receiving and sending again (all versions): in the SOS screen "Full alarm only from chosen nodes" (a list of nodes; an SOS from others arrives as an ordinary message), "Quiet hours" (from–to; then the full alarm only for nodes in a separate list of exceptions), "History of received SOS" (the last 50: sender, time, text, sender position, "Open the conversation", "Show on the map", "Clear the history") and a "Send SOS again" button when nobody confirmed the send.
* SOS, sound, file and repeating (all versions): for each node in the "Full alarm only from chosen nodes" list you choose the alarm sound (siren, bell or silent — only the window on the screen), with buttons to test each; the quiet hours have a list of exceptions of their own; the SOS history can be saved to a CSV file; the number of sends when repeating can be set to "No limit" (the SOS goes every chosen number of minutes until a send is confirmed). Android got a separate notification channel "SOS alarm (bell)".
* SOS, confirmations, texts and cleaning (all versions): an extra option "Alarm only after a second SOS" (off by default, 2–30 minutes; the main safeguard is still the hold time); on the sender's side a "Who confirmed the SOS (n)" button (with chosen nodes "X of Y") with the list of people who saw the SOS, are on the way or are calling for help; a separate SOS text for each chosen channel and node; the SOS history removes old entries by itself after the chosen number of days (7, 30, 90, 365); repeating of your own SOS stops when someone answers (an option, on by default), a notification tells who answered (an option, on by default), and the list of confirmations can be saved to a CSV file; "Calling for help" has the bell sound; the notification of a received SOS has answer buttons (Android, iPhone and iPad); on the map the nodes that answered have a ring (green, red, grey), and tapping a ring opens the node; a node that answered gets no more repeats of the SOS.
* SOS in the top bar: the button has to be held for at least 2 s (a gold fill grows while it is held), so it does not fire by accident; a short tap opens the SOS screen. A shorter time set in the SOS screen applies only to the big button in the screen, the bar always needs at least 2 s (the settings say so).
* SOS: when a chosen node is gone from the node list, the SOS screen warns about it (the alarm is still sent to it) and has a "Remove them from the choice" button.
* Computer: a new option "Start in the tray" (under "Start with the computer"): when the app starts with the computer its window stays closed and the app waits by the clock; where there is no tray the window opens after 10 s.
* What's new in the app (all versions): after an update the app shows the list of changes since the last stable version, and a new installation gets the whole list from the start (new features, improvements, fixed bugs, changes against the original). A new installation gets it only after the terms are accepted, the welcome screens are closed and the radio is configured (without a radio, after a few minutes). The window can switch between "Since the last stable" and "Everything from the start", and in MT_SW features the "What's new in the app" item opens this list at any time.
* "SOS" widget (Android and iPhone/iPad): a red plate with a yellow border and the letters SOS, in several sizes (iPhone also for the lock screen). A tap opens the SOS screen; the alarm is sent only by holding the button, so a touch in a pocket cannot send a false alarm. On the computer the "SOS" item in the tray icon menu takes its place.
* SOS: besides channels (several can be chosen) the alarm can be sent as a direct message to chosen network nodes, also ones that do not accept messages (unmonitored), with no exceptions; the nodes are chosen with a search; the confirmation counts when all the sends arrived (all versions).
* Top bar (all versions): every card now has the plate with the short name and colour of your node (also in Settings, the Assistant, the Translator and Network health) and a red "SOS" button with a yellow border that opens the SOS screen. It is on everywhere by default; in the SOS screen it can be turned off or the cards where it is shown can be chosen.
* Node neighbours (node list, neighbours icon): the window has one "Close" button (before, "Cancel" and "OK" did the same) and a new "Show on map": a map with only the node and its neighbours, with lines to them and the signal colour (SNR); when some neighbours have no position, a note under the map says how many cannot be shown (all versions).
* Desktop: "Start with the computer" fixed — on Windows the registry entry is written again at every start (so it works after the program is moved or updated) and clears the switch-off made in the Task Manager; the fault also hit the AppImage on Linux (it saved a temporary path). The course of the registration goes to the log.
* Network coverage on the main map (all versions): the layer now draws the estimated reach of every node with a position as coloured areas (purple weak signal, yellow strong; the areas lie over each other), counted like in the link planner with the shape of the terrain for a typical node (2 m antenna, 20 dBm). A node with a rounded (imprecise) position is counted from the highest ground within it (up to 2.5 km), that is from the peak. It replaces the dots from measurements, which said nothing about the reach for nodes that stay in one place. A choice of the distance the reach is counted and drawn for (from 1 to 100 km). A filter by period (when the node was heard) and by node, a "Count again" button, a colour key and an "i" description; the terrain data is downloaded and saved on the device.
* Notifications: three buttons ("Test alarm (SOS)", "Test notice from the radio", "Test new node notification"; desktop, Android, iPhone/iPad) show at once what these notifications look like.
* Message archive: the "Check how many messages would come back" button shows as a trial run how many messages would come back, how many are already there and for how many there is no channel (with the names of those channels), changing nothing.
* Node watchdog: the alert now has its own switch "Node watchdog (a node is silent)" on desktop and iPhone/iPad (Settings → MT_SW) and its own "Node watchdog" category in the Android notification settings. It no longer depends on the new node notifications, so the test notification also arrives when new nodes are switched off.
* Coverage (the track in the node details): positions heard directly are now recognised also when the radio reports no transport and no arrival time, which before left the map without dots and the track without SNR colours. When the coverage is read, the log gets the number of positions read and heard directly.
* Desktop: the program runs in one copy by default (a second start shows the window of the first one, also when it is hidden in the tray); in MT_SW features the "Allow a second copy next to it" switch lets you run several copies.
* Node watchdog: in the node details a "Send test notification" button shows at once what the silence alert looks like (with your silence time), so you can check that notifications arrive.
* Coverage map: the "Network coverage" layer switch is remembered (all versions), so the layer stays on after the app is restarted.
* Message archive: "Restore from archive" now also reads text and Markdown files (also encrypted ones): a channel by name or number, a private chat by node name, duplicates by text and second. Fix: a channel without a name (the default one) was not restored from the CSV table. The conversation titles in text and Markdown files now carry the channel name.
* SOS: the message has the alarm bell (🔔) at the start and at the end, so it arrives for the receivers as an alarm, like a message from the bell button in the chat; a position link (with a comma in the coordinates) is now clickable as a whole in all messages, not only in SOS.
* The "i" buttons in the node details have the same colour and size as the icons next to them.
* Position log: a "What the icons at the top do" card describes the mesh view, SNR, layers, route and download icons in plain words (it can be hidden).
* iPhone/iPad, message archive: choosing the folder and writing leave the time of each step in the log (making the bookmark of the choice, resolving it, writing the file), so that what makes the app stall when the folder is chosen can be found.
* Notifications, more categories (desktop, iPhone/iPad): three new switches — "Alarm notifications" (messages with the bell, e.g. an SOS from a neighbour), "Notices from the radio" and "Update notifications" (the app and the radio software). On iPhone they are also in Settings → MT_SW and are kept in step with the app. On by default.
* Android, notifications: the settings have a separate "Notifications" section with the row "Notifications (in system settings)", which opens the system notification page of the app (sound, vibration, importance and lock screen for each kind).
* Message archive, restoring (all versions): the "Restore from archive" button reads the messages from CSV files (also ones encrypted with the same password) back into the history. The archive saves the channel name, so a message goes to the channel with the same name even after the channels were swapped around; private messages return to their own conversation, and messages already in the app are not duplicated. Works from the CSV format only.
* Message archive, password protection (all versions): the archive files can be encrypted with a password (AES-256, key from the password via PBKDF2-SHA256, 100,000 rounds) — they get the ending .enc, and a file also opens with `openssl enc -d -aes-256-ctr -pbkdf2 -md sha256 -iter 100000`. New "Password protection" section on the archive screen (set and remove the password), description in the "i" window, MT_SW features, documentation and assistant knowledge updated.
* Coverage map from measurements (all versions): in a node's position log the SNR button colours the track on the map by the signal strength the radio received each position with (direct packets only), with a colour scale; the layers button saves the track as a coloured KML file to load as a map layer.
* Comparing two tracks (all versions): in a node's position log the route button picks a second node and draws its track beside the first, in another colour; the dialog shows the length and number of positions of both tracks.
* Node watchdog: quiet hours (one setting for all nodes, the notification waits until they end) and one shared notification when several nodes (three or more) go silent at once.
* SOS: shortcuts that open the SOS screen — a tile in the Android quick settings, a "SOS MT_SW" shortcut (Siri, Shortcuts, the action button) on iPhone/iPad and an item in the computer's tray menu. The alarm is still sent only after holding the button.
* "i" buttons with a plain description (what it is for and how to use it, a scrollable window) on the new functions: node watchdog, node availability, SOS, message archive, and coverage and track comparison in the position log.
* Coverage of the whole network (all versions): in Network health, in the Summary, "Save coverage of all nodes (KML)" saves the SNR measurements of all nodes together as a KML file (a folder per node, coloured dots) to load as a map layer.
* Message archive (all versions): in "MT_SW features" an automatic copy of the text messages in a folder you choose — Android (folder pick with the access kept), desktop (a directory) and iPhone/iPad (a folder from the Files app, remembered as a bookmark). Format: text, CSV or Markdown; one file per day or per conversation and day; updated every 5 minutes to 3 hours; a "Save now" button. iPhone/iPad: choosing a folder now works too (it did nothing before). Deep link `message-archive`.
* SOS mode (all versions): in "MT_SW features" an "SOS" screen with a big button — held (time to choose: tap, 1, 2, 3, 5 s) it sends an emergency message with the position to the chosen channel and shows the delivery confirmation. Options: channel, text, adding the position (phone, or the node when there is none), repeating every 1–30 minutes up to 50 times with a stop after a confirmation; it goes on after the screen is closed. Described in "MT_SW features", the settings search and the assistant's knowledge; deep link `sos`.
* Network report (all versions): the "Network summary" export (PDF, HTML, Markdown, text, CSV) now has a node list — signal, battery, when last heard and distance from our node — and a position sketch of the nodes with north up (a drawing in PDF and HTML, coordinates in the text formats).
* Node availability history (all versions): in the node details, below the node watchdog, a "Node availability" card with a bar chart of the recorded packets from the node — range: a day, 3, 7, 14 or 30 days; under the chart the number of time slots in which the node was heard and its longest silence. It reaches back as far as the log retention. Described in "MT_SW features", the settings search and the assistant's knowledge.
* Node watchdog (all versions): in the node details, below the notes, a "Node watchdog" card — per node, a notification when the radio has not heard it for the chosen time: from 1 hour up to 30 days (ready buttons) or your own time in hours up to 90 days. The notification disappears when the node is heard. The "Also count packets passing through the node" option (off by default) also counts a packet relayed by this node as a sign of life. It works while connected to the radio on Android, desktop and iPhone/iPad; described in "MT_SW features", the settings search and the assistant's knowledge.
* iPhone/iPad, QR codes: "Connections" now has the QR scanner icon (like on Android) — it scans the connect code, and when importing also channels and contacts. It uses the phone camera (iOS asks for permission; after a refusal the app shows a notice and opens the app settings). The permission text in Info.plist mentions QR scanning (after changing `iosApp/project.yml` run xcodegen in the `iosApp` folder).
* iPhone/iPad, event theme: the event colors and branding and the "Event theme" switch now work the same as on Android.
* iPhone/iPad, map: map layer import (KML, KMZ, GeoJSON) with the "Add layer" button in the layers sheet — the file is chosen in the Files app; before, the button did nothing.
* iPhone/iPad, map: KMZ files are now unpacked (the KML and overlay images from the archive) like on Android; before, a KMZ gave no layer on iPhone.
* iPhone/iPad, terrain view (HUD): enabled — the "Terrain view" entries in the node menu, details and on the map are shown when the phone has a camera and motion sensors (camera, heading and pressure were already there, only the switch was missing).
* iPhone/iPad, terrain view: saving the camera picture with the overlaid nodes to the Photos app works (the button like on Android); iOS asks for permission to add photos (after changing `iosApp/project.yml` run xcodegen).
* iPhone/iPad, screen and permissions: the screen stays on during firmware updates, scanning and the terrain view ("keep screen on" did nothing on iPhone before); the QR code dialog sets full brightness and restores the old value when closed; the location permission shows the real state (it was always "not required" before), with a request button and a shortcut to the app settings.
* iPhone/iPad, Bluetooth: when Bluetooth is switched off in the system, the Connections screen shows the same warning as on Android (it never appeared on iPhone before); the system "turn on Bluetooth" alert is left out.
* iPhone/iPad, notifications: the notification permission shows the real system state (allowed / not asked yet / refused), with a request button and a shortcut to the app settings — before, it was always "not required".
* iPhone/iPad, notification settings like on Android: in the app settings, instead of five switches there is one row "Notifications (in iPhone settings)" that opens the app page in the iPhone Settings; there (Settings → MT_SW) are the event switches (message, new nodes, low battery, reactions, waypoints, sound while the app is open). The app takes a change over at once, and changes made by the app itself show up there.
* iPhone, lock screen: the statistics widget has three small lock screen sizes — round (nodes online), rectangular (node name, number online and two statistics lines) and one line.
* iPhone/iPad, Siri and Shortcuts: a new shortcut "MT_SW network status" (with Siri: "Network status in MT_SW_APP") reads out our node name and the basic statistics — the same data as the widget.
* iPhone/iPad, language and region: a change of the system language or region is now noticed at once (dates, units and numbers refresh without restarting the app).
* iPhone/iPad, gaps compared with Android: the node position settings have the "Set fixed position from phone" button; the security settings have working "Backup Keys", "Restore Keys" and "Delete Key Backup" (backups kept in the Keychain, like on Android); the external notification settings can import an RTTTL ringtone from a file and play it; numbers (e.g. temperature, altitude) use the decimal and grouping separators of the language set in the phone (e.g. a comma in Polish) instead of always a dot.
* Node details, commands (all platforms): the GPIO write now waits for the node's confirmation and shows a small notification — "Delivery confirmed" or "No response from node"; a quick command (e.g. /ping) shows "Command sent", or an error message when the radio rejects it. These two commands used to give no hint whether they went out. The GPIO read and remote favorite/ignore/add contact already showed a result.
* Database (all platforms): writes are faster — the database (in WAL mode) no longer waits for the disk after every single write (`synchronous = NORMAL`, safe against file corruption; a crash of the whole phone can cost at most the last few writes, closing only the app costs nothing). Set only when the file really runs in WAL mode. Reading the widget sizes on the settings screen (Android, iPhone/iPad) now happens off the main thread.
* Logs (iPhone/iPad): writing to the file is much faster — the file of the log part stays open (it used to be opened and closed for every single line), and the date formatting uses one shared formatter instead of creating a new one for every line; every line still goes to the file at once, so nothing is lost when the system closes the app. Saving the logs to the chosen folder when the app goes to the background now runs off the main thread (packing large logs used to block the move to the background, which the system could take for a hang and close the app), and the savings no longer overlap.
* Statistics widget (Android): looks like the iPhone one — group and section headings in the MT_SW gold, in capitals; spacing between groups and lines; a thin line between the sections (our node, favorite, other); spare room is shared evenly between the sections instead of an empty strip at the bottom.
* Buildings and forests (panorama, planner): the first Overpass server is now the OpenStreetMap France instance, which returns the same pieces in 4–5 s (overpass-api.de answered after 30–40 s even to an empty query); overpass-api.de and the former backup servers stay as backups; the log names the server for every piece.
* Panorama and planner, buildings: pieces that have already arrived are used at once — also when the download was interrupted or did not finish (the panorama used to say the downloaded buildings do not cover the place although part of the data was already on the device); downloading again continues where it stopped. Instead of a fixed 12 minutes the buildings download goes on as long as new pieces keep arriving (it gives up after 15 minutes without a new piece). When a server does not answer in time, a backup server is asked before the piece is cut into smaller ones (each smaller one costs another wait). Planner coverage takes buildings from the area saved by the panorama when it holds the whole radius.
* Map (iPhone/iPad): fixed the cause of the shifted tapping — the map is a native view drawn in real screen points, while the app computed its size in the density that includes the interface scale, so the picture was larger than the coordinates used for hit testing (the error grew away from the top-left corner); the map now gets the screen's real density, which also fixes taps on clusters, waypoints and tracks. In addition, taps are converted if the view and map sizes ever differ (node bubbles, short and long taps); the log carries the view size, map size and scale.
* Map (iPhone): when a node bubble is tapped, the app checks the scale in which the map reports screen positions and corrects the tap area; the log now carries the tap geometry (position, scale, nearest dot).
* Firmware update over Bluetooth (iPhone/iPad): the device in update mode (nRF52 DFU, ESP32 OTA) is found without a MAC address, because iOS only gives a device its own identifier.
* Widget (all platforms): a node's temperature and humidity on one line, side by side (like voltage and current), instead of separate rows.
* Propagation: the "Keep the largest area" option (on by default) — the forecast area only grows and does not shrink on zooming in, also on a manual download; kept across restarts.
* Propagation: several areas instead of one (replacing the "Keep the largest area" option): "Add area for this view" with a choice of detail (normal, detailed, very detailed), up to 8 areas that may overlap (the one with the finer grid is drawn on top, without double darkening), each removed separately, "Update all areas" and automatic refresh download all of them at once, each within its own bounds; areas are saved on the device (the old single forecast becomes the first area) (Android, iPhone/iPad, desktop).
* Propagation: areas have their own names (a "Name of the new area" field; unnamed ones are "Area 1, 2…"), and with several of them thin outlines of each are drawn on the map, so it is clear where they lie and where they overlap.
* Panorama: the buildings download starts from small pieces (5 km), because large ones (30 km) exceeded the server's time and were cut smaller only after a minute, so it took very long; the log now has every piece (time, object count or the cause of a failure).
* Stats widget (Android): the layout is worked out from the room the data needs (shrinking the widget no longer drops the third section while the data fits, and the empty space at the bottom goes); the row height follows the system font; lines too long (e.g. "Traffic: TX / RX (D: …)" and "Diagnostics: …") are cut into several rows instead of ending in "…".
* Map (iPhone): node bubbles are matched to the tap on the screen instead of in a box handed to the map (some bubbles had a shifted tap area).
* App icon: new option "Flat icon (no 3D)" for all styles (Android, iPhone/iPad, desktop); the earlier flat icon is back as a tick beside the style.
* App icon: "Flat icon (no 3D)" is now a real checkbox at the top of the dialog (one for all styles), not an item in the style list.
* App icon on Android: removed the blurry shadow around the lettering and outline (it made the icon look soft); the background gradient and gloss stay.
* Map (iPhone, Android, desktop): the map layers no longer rebuild on every frame of a pan or zoom — the visible area is held still until the camera leaves it. This was the main cause of the map stutter on the iPhone.
* Panorama: the buildings download carries on after leaving the screen or the app (it used to be cancelled silently, so buildings were never saved); cancellation and errors go to the log only, with no error message in the window.
* Stats widget (Android): a solid rounded background so the text is readable on any wallpaper; taller rows (fit a larger system font) and a group heading no longer hangs alone at the bottom of a column.
* Map: the map button on the bar always returns to the view the map opened with (after zooming out or panning too, not only after flying in on a node).
* Propagation: the widest downloaded area is remembered (e.g. zoom out fully and press "download for this view"); refreshes fetch that whole area, not just the screen. It only grows when the view leaves it.
* Map: a tap on a bubble now lands exactly on the drawn bubble (tap area and bubble centre computed from its real position above the node).
* Panorama: the automatic quality steps down only after really long stutter (longer measuring window, higher threshold).
* Panorama: new texture for mountains and forests — soft, directionless blotches instead of vertical streaks, smooth slope shading.
* Panorama: buildings keep their proportions (height scaled at draw time, no fixed cap), width matches height.
* Panorama: buildings are lower relative to the mountains (capped height), terrain exaggerated more, and building drawing much faster (bands built once, off-screen ones skipped).
* Propagation map: full legend text (no clipping).
* Map: tapped bubble chosen by distance first, wider tap area.
* Widget: voltage and current on one line, with temperature, humidity and pressure below, using the same short labels as the node details list.
* Widget: its data is rebuilt at most every 3 s (before, on every received packet), which takes load off the app.
* Panorama: mountain ridges are smoothed (they flow like real ones, without the steps of the elevation data) and slopes have deeper, more three-dimensional shading. The outline of a band is worked out once per band, which also speeds up drawing.
* Panorama: mist lying in the valleys of the farther bands, a subtle slope texture (gullies and ribs) and warmer, stronger light when the sun is low (sunrise and sunset).
* Panorama: a rock texture on the mountains (a fine grain with vertical gully streaks), stronger on near ridges and fading with distance.
* Panorama: forests on the lower slopes of the near ridges (dark, grainy patches) and a subtle texture on buildings.
* Panorama: a "Panorama quality" setting: "Automatic" (default: starts at the best and steps down by itself when the picture stutters), "Best" (textures, forests, building texture), "Very good" (rock texture) and "Good" (light, up to 800 buildings).
