A manual choice turns the automatic mode off and is remembered.
* Map: the zoom level for the node and contour layers is passed on only when a whole level changes, not on every frame of zooming (fewer recalculations and less stutter).
* Documentation and the assistant's knowledge updated with the panorama quality, the antenna direction and tilt, and the new widget lines.
* Panorama: a short notice "Panorama quality lowered automatically" when the automatic mode steps down. The panorama and the map write frame statistics to the logs (only windows with slow frames on the map), which helps to find where it stutters.
* Planner: the antenna direction (azimuth in compass degrees) can always be typed, and a new "Antenna tilt" field (up/down) affects the link and the coverage.
* All versions: on first start (after installing, after updating to this version and after erasing the data) a “Terms of use and data consent” window appears before the setup wizard: a long text saying that node data, messages, logs and statistics may reach the Internet and be publicly visible, that the authors do not collect them, that they are not liable, and that claims are waived to the extent permitted by law (in any country). The “I agree” button unlocks only after the text is scrolled to the end and a 10-second countdown. “I do not agree” closes the app (for someone with data from an earlier version it first asks whether to erase all data). The date of consent is stored and shown in “About” → “Terms and data consent” (where the terms can also be read again); MT_SW features has only a shortcut to that place. The text of the terms can be copied, and when it changes (a new text version) the window appears again for everyone; the text version is stored together with the date. The same section has “Erase all app data” (a separate warning with a 10-second countdown), which returns the app to its fresh-install state and withdraws the consent.
* All versions: the planner has a "Compare results" card: "Remember this result" stores the distance, the margin both ways, the SF, the frequency and the antenna heights, and the list (up to 12, kept on the device) lets you compare a change of antenna, height or radio with what it was before.
* All versions: the terrain panorama has haze on the farther bands, light from the real position of the sun (dimmed at night), and a tap on a ridge shows its distance, height above sea level and angle above the horizon. Map: with the propagation layer on, a small legend and the forecast hour slider sit in the corner.
* All versions: the terrain panorama has more depth: the terrain bands have a gradient from a light ridge to a dark foot, slopes are shaded by which way they face (light from the left) and the ridge has a light line, so hills read as bodies and not as flat cut-outs.
* All versions: the terrain panorama draws buildings as real bodies from the OpenStreetMap outline: walls seen at an angle (narrower and darker when they stand sideways), a light roof and a thin outline, and farther buildings fade like the terrain. Without saved buildings the panorama draws columns as before.
* All versions: planner — the woods and built-up land from OpenStreetMap for the coverage are now saved on the device for small areas too (before, only for large ones), so a coverage calculated once works later without the internet.
* All versions: planner — with ground reflections on, "Show on map" adds a separate "Planner: ground reflection" layer: the two legs of the reflected ray, the reflection point and the gain in dB. The layer can be hidden without losing the plan.
* All versions: the favorite and other node cards in the widget also show the voltage, the time since the last activity, the number of hops, and the device role and model. Android: the widget switches nodes every minute, as on the iPhone.
* All versions: planner — "Show on map" adds the buildings as layers of their own: for a route the buildings along it (those blocking the line of sight in red), for a coverage the buildings in range as a backdrop (only tall ones from 15 km); a switch in the radio settings, on by default.
* All versions: in the terrain panorama buildings are drawn as solids: the wall darkens toward the foot, a lighter roof on top, a dark edge where the height steps, and farther ones fade toward the sky.
* Android and iPhone/iPad: in the statistics widget the corner shows our node's chip (its colour and short name, as on the map) instead of the online count; in the bigger widgets the other nodes' cards have the full name on one side and the chip with the short name on the other.
* All versions: a new map layer "Propagation (weather forecast)" in the layers settings: a coloured 24-hour forecast shows where the weather extends the radio range (from temperature, humidity and pressure, Open-Meteo, CC BY 4.0). Choice of kind, transparency, hour, "Download/Update for this view", saved on the device and automatic refresh.
* All versions: planner and antenna calculator — antenna kinds (omni, Yagi with a number of elements, Moxon, panel, biquad, helix, dish, sector) with a radiation pattern; the planner takes antenna directivity and aiming into account also in the range all around, has separate ground reflection and building and tree reflection options, and the link result shows the reflection gain and the directivity loss.
* All versions: remote administration — the request for the MT_SW_FIRMWARE version and for the local statistics configuration now also goes out to the remote node (before it never went out, so the card was shown as unsupported).
* Android and iPhone/iPad: tapping overlapping nodes on the map shows a list of all the nodes under the finger instead of opening a random one.
* All versions: the planner uses the buildings and terrain saved on the device without asking the internet; data older than the chosen number of days (30 by default, the choice now also for terrain) is refreshed by itself, and kept as it is when there is no internet.
* All versions: once terrain and buildings are downloaded the button becomes "Update"; buildings refresh only pieces older than the chosen number of days (7, 14, 30, 60 or 90; 30 by default), and terrain fetches only the missing tiles.
* All versions: the buildings download (terrain panorama) saves every finished piece on the device at once, so after an interruption the next download fetches only the missing pieces instead of starting over.
* All versions: in the widget the statistics of our node have a fixed layout: network and radio in the left column, node, resources and connections in the right one. This allows bigger text.
* All versions: the "Statistics widget" settings have a description that the widget can be made bigger, and the content options show only for the sizes the widget really has on the screen (smallest: one option, bigger: an extra one). The app detects the widget sizes itself on iPhone, iPad and Android.
* iPhone and iPad: the choice of what the widget shows in its sizes (our node, favorites or the whole network) is now in the app settings ("Statistics widget"), the same as on Android. Node cards fill the whole area with bigger text.
* All versions: in the terrain panorama the buildings now have their own orange colour and show as something standing above the ground, instead of blending into it.
* All versions: logs are saved in the chosen folder as two separate files: "mt_sw_logi_24h.zip" (last 24 hours) and "mt_sw_logi_pelne.zip" (full log). On desktop the files are refreshed in the logs folder every few minutes.
* All versions: the "Synchronize statistics automatically" switch now has its own description (what it does and when).
* All versions: the terrain panorama has a "Buildings" option (off by default): the skyline is raised by the buildings of OpenStreetMap. You download them once, around the connected radio (every building up to 15 km, only the tall ones up to 100 km) and they are kept on the device like the terrain, so opening the panorama stays quick; one button deletes them.
* iPhone and iPad: tapping a node bubble (and a track point, waypoint, or a node on the secondary maps) opens that node again; iPhone passed the number as a decimal, which the app refused.
* All versions: the app icon is livelier and has depth: a background gradient, a gloss at the top, a shadow and raised edges on the mark (standard and dark icons; clear and tinted unchanged).
* Android: the widget has its own choice in MT_SW features ("Adaptive widget"): the whole mesh or one node, apart from the notification. The statistics on the widget are arranged in groups with headings, like on iPhone.
* Android: removed the widgets we did not add ("Local stats" and "Node tracker"). Only the MT_SW widget remains.
* All versions: the widget statistics are arranged in groups: network (nodes, unread), the node (power, uptime), radio (airtime, traffic, diagnostics), resources (CPU, RAM, flash, PSRAM) and connections (Wi-Fi, Bluetooth). On iPhone and iPad the widget shows them in sections with gold headings, in two balanced columns.
* iPhone and iPad: the widget can be made bigger (touch and hold the widget → "Edit widget", or change its size). Medium shows your choice: our node, favorites in turn or the whole network in turn (every minute). Large has our node on top and, below, your choice of favorites or the whole network in turn. Extra large (4x6, in the new iPhone system and on iPad) shows our node, favorites below it and the rest of the network under them (without favorites and without our node). Texts are bigger and fitted to the space.
* Android: the widget has three sizes, like on iPhone: 4x2 shows your choice of our node, or favorites, or the whole network in turn (always starting with the full statistics of our node), 4x4 has our node on top and below it favorites or the whole network in turn, and 4x6 shows our node, favorites and the rest of the network. The choice is in MT_SW features → Adaptive widget ("Small widget shows", "Second area of the bigger widgets") with a description of all sizes.
* All versions: new switch in MT_SW features "Skip readings with a wrong time in charts" (off by default; on leaves out readings from a node with a wrong clock, clock icon).
* All versions: the widget shows the noise floor with the amplifier (LNA) correction, like the app and the notification.
* All versions: in the theme picker the category names are gold and larger than the options.
* iPhone and iPad: the "Updated" label and time on the widget follow the app's language, not the phone's.
* iPhone and iPad: in the assistant settings the "Free memory" row now shows which model is in memory (it always said none before), and tapping it frees the model, as on Android and desktop.
* All versions: the MT_SW features screen now keeps real settings ("Looks, widget and data": scale, theme, icon, widget, statistics sync, font) apart from "Shortcuts", which only open settings elsewhere. The description of "Device statistics sync" is expanded: what it is for, an example, what is synchronized, what it leaves alone and when it runs.
* All versions: the new "LAN statistics" query in On-demand diagnostics (MT_SW_FIRMWARE v3 and newer): the node's IP address on Wi-Fi or Ethernet, UDP on the local network, MQTT and the broker connection. The result is in the report too.
* All versions: remote administration now asks a remote node for its MT_SW_FIRMWARE version and statistics settings too. Before it asked only the connected radio, so a remote node was treated as not supporting the feature.
* All versions: on the Connections screen the "Radio setup" row is shown only while the radio is not configured yet (no region; then as "Set your region"). It used to take room all the time.
* iPhone and iPad: when an AI model (e.g. Gemma) does not fit in memory, the app no longer gives up at once but retries with a smaller text window and, as a last resort, on the processor only.
* iPhone and iPad: the adaptive display is now described as a widget ("Adaptive widget", "Track on the widget"), because the iPhone has no permanent notification. In MT_SW features the "Statistics widget" item now has its own choice: the whole mesh or one node. Yesterday and Day before yesterday in the activity list start with a capital letter.
* iPhone and iPad: the app now tells the system all its languages (it declared only English before), so system prompts such as the clipboard paste question show in the phone's language, Polish included.
* All versions: the map filter has two new limits: "Max hops" (no limit or 0-7) and "Max distance from my node" (no limit or 1-250 km). Idea from the Meshtastic app for Apple. The settings are remembered, also apply to the network view (HUD) and count towards the active-filter dot.
* All versions: tapping several nodes standing on the same spot on the map opens a "pick a node" list (as for a cluster), because their chips overlap and only the top one could be opened before.
* All versions: network health charts (e.g. noise) skip readings from a node with a wrong clock: more than 30 days in the future, more than a year older than the newest, and repeated timestamps. Tables and exports keep the full data.
* All versions: the theme picker has categories (dark, light, system-matching, other) and shorter theme names; "min" instead of "minute"; in the node list an inactive node shows "yesterday" and "day before yesterday" instead of "1 day ago" and "2 days ago".
* iPhone and iPad: the local TAK server works the same as on Android and desktop. It listens on 127.0.0.1:8089 only, with TLS and mutual authentication (only the client certificate issued by this install is accepted), creates its own certificates on first start and exports the same data package for ATAK/iTAK.
* Desktop (macOS, Windows, Linux): new "Start with the computer" option in MT_SW features — starts the app at login (off by default, works in the installed app). Android already starts after a phone restart; iPhone and iPad cannot start by themselves.
* All versions: new "Merge data from a backup" in Backup and restore. Pick a backup file (from any system) and the app completes the statistics only of nodes known to both it and the backup, adding no new nodes and needing no restart. Backups work the same on Android, desktop and iPhone/iPad (one format), so data can move from an old phone to a new one.
* All versions: new "Device statistics sync" in MT_SW features. Every radio has its own database; the sync completes in both databases the telemetry and position history and the latest readings of the nodes both databases know (no duplicates: a packet heard by both radios counts once). Nodes known to one database only are not copied to the other; notes, names, favourites, ignore and mute stay unchanged. It runs automatically (an option, off by default) after the radio changes, or with the "Sync now" button.
* iPhone and iPad: every next message (reaction, alert) from the same conversation rings and vibrates again, on the watch too. Before, a new notification silently replaced the previous one, because the system does not alert for a replaced notification. Now the notifications of one conversation stack, and reading the conversation clears the whole stack.
* iPhone and iPad: checking for new app versions (like on Android and desktop): a minute after the start and then twice a day, with a notification and a window with a button to the releases page; switched on and off in the update settings.
* iPhone, iPad and desktop: the Discovery report now exports to PDF (text only before), like on Android. Letters outside Western European (e.g. Polish "ł") are written without accents in the PDF.
* Android: new "MT_SW statistics" widget (4x2) with the same lines as the permanent notification (the whole mesh or one tracked node, depending on the adaptive display). The data for the Android and iPhone widgets now comes from one shared piece of code, so they show exactly the same.
* iPhone and iPad: the assistant refreshes its knowledge about the app from the internet by itself (once a day, only when the assistant's internet access is on; files are checked with a checksum), like on Android and desktop. The phone's location (CoreLocation) is now available to the whole app, e.g. for the assistant's LoRa region advice.
* iPhone and iPad: the full AI assistant like on Android and desktop: the same tools (guide, showing settings and nodes on the map, message draft, web search and reading pages), memory, history, self-test (`/test`, `/sprzet`) and mesh data. The assistant code is now shared by all systems; on iPhone the internet goes through the system network client (https only, no local addresses), and test result files go to the app's documents folder.
* iPhone and iPad: the "MT_SW statistics" widget (medium size, 4x2) with the same statistics as the permanent adaptive notification on Android (one shared text code for every system). It replaces that notification on iPhone; the adaptive display (the whole mesh or one node) now works on iPhone too. Described in MT_SW features → Statistics widget. The widget reads its data through a shared folder (App Group).
* iPhone and iPad: the app asks the system for a higher memory limit (the "increased memory limit" entitlement) so large AI models (for example Gemma 4 E2B) can load. When a model fails to load, the error now shows the device memory.
* iPhone and iPad: the app icon has variants for the system icon styles (iOS 18 and later): dark (black background, outline and lettering yellow) and tinted (black background, light drawing that the system colours). Also a new "App icon" setting in MT_SW features (all versions): Automatic (default; on iPhone per the system style), Standard (as before), Dark, Clear, Tinted. Android changes the home screen icon, iPhone uses alternate icons (on iPhone "clear" is light, because iOS does not accept transparent icons), the desktop app changes the window and Dock/taskbar icon.
* All versions: tapping a notification about a message, reaction or alert opens the conversation it came from. Android already did this; now iPhone/iPad and the desktop apps (macOS, Windows and Linux) do the same, and the app window comes to the front.
* All versions (Android, desktop, iPhone/iPad): the bottom buttons (tabs) open a page where you left it. Only pressing the button of the page that is already open brings it back to its first screen (from a conversation to the conversation list, from node details, settings or the map to their main screen). On the node list that second press also clears the search and scrolls to the top; the conversation list scrolls to the top too.
* All versions (Android, desktop, iPhone/iPad): the "last heard" time (node list, details, map, adaptive notification, TalkBack description) now follows the language set in the app, e.g. "15 min ago", "2 h ago", "3 days ago"; anything older than 7 days shows a date such as "November 7". Before, desktop and iPhone always showed English "ago", and Android used the phone's system language instead of the app's.
* iPhone and iPad: the assistant no longer shows the model's empty thinking marks (`<think>`) at the top of an answer. The `/sprzet` command works on iPhone too: it shows the model, its size and memory, where it runs (processor or GPU), the context window, and the device's system, cores and memory.
* iPhone and iPad: the oldest supported system is now iOS 17 (it was 16). The AI engine library needed iOS 16.4 anyway, which gave a build warning; the warning about the missing library identifier is gone too.
* iPhone and iPad: message notifications play a sound again when the phone is locked or the app is in the background. The app used to treat itself as always open, so every notification was quiet (the sound played only on the watch). A new setting "Notification sound while the app is open" (Settings → Notifications), off by default. The system sound is used; the volume follows the phone's ringer volume.
* Bluetooth signal of the phone connected to a node (MT_SW_FIRMWARE, ESP32 and nRF52): the app reads the new "ble_rssi" field and treats it like the WiFi signal. The node list gets its own chip with a Bluetooth icon and the label "BLE" next to Wi-Fi (shown only while a phone is connected to the node), "Signal quality" gets its own chart line, an (i) description, list cards and CSV export, "Network health" (Signal tab) gets a separate "BLE RSSI" chart, and "On-demand diagnostics" a new "Bluetooth RSSI" row. The value is also in the adaptive notification and in the assistant's knowledge. When a node starts sending one signal instead of the other, the values swap; when neither arrives, the last known one stays. It works the same on Android, desktop and iPhone/iPad.
* Logs: the Bluetooth connection no longer writes a separate line for every received and sent packet (hundreds of entries per second, which made the real error hard to find). Instead there is one summary per minute (packets and bytes); events, errors and connection changes are logged as before.
* Merge with the original (another small one): the Dokka 2.3.0 documentation tool and refreshed app startup profiles. Nothing changes in how the app works or in our features.
* All versions (Android, computer, iPhone/iPad): new "Saturated" and "Very saturated" themes, each in three versions (light or dark like the system, light, dark). The same palette with stronger colours in every hue (hue and lightness unchanged, so gold stays gold), to pick in the theme settings. On iPhone/iPad and the computer "Saturated" is now the default, because those systems have no wallpaper colours and the fixed palette looked pale; the old look stays as "Light", "Dark" and "System default".
* iPhone and iPad: the map layers sheet now has offline map downloads (the area on screen), like on Android.
* All versions (Android, computer, iPhone/iPad): the theme picker is grouped into "Dark", "Light", "Like the system" and "Other" (Dynamic), with short names inside the groups ("Standard", "Saturated", "Very saturated", "Black and white"). Times in the settings use the short "min" instead of "minute/minutes", and "last heard" (node list, details, notification) shows "yesterday" and "day before yesterday" instead of "1 day ago" and "2 days ago".
* iPhone and iPad: a new "Keep running in the background" setting (MT_SW features → Work in the background), off by default. The system suspends an app that is not on screen and cuts the Wi-Fi/TCP link to the radio (also over Tailscale); once on, the app keeps running in the background with background location, like the original Meshtastic app for Apple. The position is read rarely and is not sent anywhere. Needs the "Always" permission.
* iPhone and iPad: the radio connection survives the app being closed in the background. Bluetooth state restoration is on, so when the system closes the app and the radio comes back in range, the system starts the app again and the connection resumes (before, the connection was gone after a background close until the app was opened by hand).
* Merge with the original — version 2.8.5: app version number 2.8.5, newer Android build tools (AGP 9.5.1, android tools 32.5.1), refreshed hardware and firmware data, translations and store screenshots. Our Polish translations are unchanged. MT_SW_APP release v5(2.8.5).test.1.
* Build: adapted to the new Gradle (the `androidApp` configuration no longer reaches into the root project), iOS native library sharing enabled (cinterop commonization).
* Logs on iPhone/iPad work like on Android: a switch for collecting logs (off by default), a separate switch for the automatic log of the last 24 hours (on by default), every entry with the date and time to the millisecond, the log in consecutive parts of about 400 KB (`mt_sw_log_<session>_<number>.txt`), older parts replaced, the crash cause in its own file a choice of a folder in the Files app that gets a fresh zip of the logs on its own (at start, every few minutes and when the app goes to the background; instead of the Downloads folder, which the iPhone does not have), app lifecycle events and memory warnings, the thread name in every line, an entry on how the earlier run ended (in the background, in the foreground, normal close), the AI engine's own logs (llama, whisper) in the package, the same log detail level as on Android, a richer crash record, orientation changes and a periodic resource entry, and a report package to send on demand (Discord, WhatsApp, Signal). The in-memory log on desktop (Debug screen) also has the time on every entry.
* Assistant compatibility test: the dictation check uses the app language, the screen stays on during the test, and the NPU and graphics card runs share one conversation (one file with the whole chat). Tips reworded.
* Assistant compatibility test: the intro text now explains why the test matters (improving the assistant on many different devices).
* Assistant compatibility test: the running-test window shows rotating short tips about app features (shared on all platforms).
* Assistant compatibility test: before the start a large window describes every step exactly and needs a confirmation, and a second one asks separately for consent to download and try the dictation model (the test can also run without dictation). The assistant gets a longer general question with no ready-made answer, so the model has to think. While the test runs the window stays on screen and the only thing you can do is stop the test. The test clears the remembered failed model starts by itself at the start. When the model runs on the NPU and there were no errors, the question and the hardware report are repeated on the graphics card (NPU skipped) and a comparison is added to the package; the setting then returns to what it was.
* AI assistant: the "AI answer" note is blue, from the app's standard palette, so it is easier to see.
* Tablet and desktop: in the chats view the conversation list is narrower and the message channel wider (about 36% to 64%). Nodes and the documentation keep their usual split. Choosing another conversation replaces the open one instead of stacking on it, so channels can be switched freely. The width can still be changed by dragging.
* iPhone and iPad: the link to the radio comes back by itself after a break (for example overnight). When the radio is not seen in a scan, the app leaves a long connection request with the system (up to 30 minutes, repeated), which the system finishes in the background as soon as the radio is in range again, instead of scanning every few seconds.
* Traceroute history: every entry says whether the trace was requested manually or received automatically. In sniffer mode, responses addressed to other nodes are also recorded as observed (with no alert); an alert is raised only for our own requests.
* AI-made content is marked: assistant answers carry the label "AI answer, it may contain mistakes", and translations (in chat and in the translator) the suffix "(AI)". Applies to every build.
* Chat pictures: the consent window before sending explains where the picture goes (third-party services, how long it is kept, IP address, who can see it); the texts are in the PL and EN resources. Added a privacy policy (PRIVACY.md) and a NOTICE file.
* The Android store build (built as "google", for Google Play) no longer has any Google services: it uses the same code and the same map (MapLibre) as F-Droid, without Google Maps, Firebase (crashes and analytics), Datadog, ML Kit, Gemini Nano, ARCore and Google fonts. The QR scanner is ZXing in both builds. The Gradle task names are unchanged.
* Removed the NLLB-200 translator model (CC BY-NC licence, non-commercial use only, incompatible with GPL-3.0): translation now uses freely licensed models (Gemma, Qwen, Bielik); NLLB files downloaded earlier are deleted at start. Its separate engine (ONNX Runtime) and all NLLB translator code were removed with it: the app is smaller. For the same reason (Llama and Gemma Terms of Use licences) the TranslateGemma 4B and 12B, DeepSeek on Llama (8B and 70B) and Llama 3.2 1B models were removed; their files are deleted at start too.
* Android: the installer and the system settings now show our release name (for example `v4(2.8.4).test.1`), the same as the About screen, without the build number and the store name (fdroid, google).
* AI models: "Other" is always last, after all the families. Gemma comes first.
* Translator: it has one command (`/tłumacz`, the translation test, with or without Polish letters; the other commands are only in the assistant), an "i" icon with the description of the command and a session history under the clock icon. Every session (translations together with the results of the commands) is saved on the device and can be opened, deleted or started anew.

### v4 (2.8.4) and earlier

#### November 2026 (up to 4 November)

* Tablet and desktop: the side bar with the buttons (Messages, Nodes, Settings, Connect and others) keeps its buttons at the bottom of the screen, and a new function is added above them. On the iPad the bar no longer overlaps the clock and battery at the top.
* Sending chat pictures: when the main host (catbox.moe) refuses files (the "412 uploads paused" error), the app tries the backup hosts on its own (litterbox.catbox.moe, then uguu.se). Pictures on the backup hosts stay for a shorter time (72 hours or a few hours).
* New "Support the project" entry in "MT_SW features" and in "About": opens the developer's page (Ko-fi) in the browser for voluntary support of the maintenance and development of the app. It is also in the settings search and in the assistant's knowledge.
* Metric screens (device, signal, environment, power, position and others): the chart is always on top and the list below it, also on tablets and desktop, so the chart is no longer squeezed into a narrow column.
* iPhone and iPad: the base of the app for iPhone in the same repository: nodes, messages, connections and settings tabs, Bluetooth and Wi-Fi to the radio (with automatic search on the network). Map, notifications, picking and saving pictures, the microphone, GPS, compass and barometer work; AI assistant, translator and dictation (llama.cpp), opening links and maps, sharing text, firmware file picker, assistant chats and notes saved on the iPhone, radio firmware update from the iPhone (download and unpack), interface scale, opening links (channels, contacts), the welcome screen and image loading; AI features come later. Also on iPhone: device icons with correct colours, the 3D device view, the terrain panorama view, the "Force stop app" button.
* The DeepSeek models (1.5B, 7B, Llama 8B, 14B) now use Q4_0 files like the other models, because the Q4_K_M files gave nonsense on the NPU and graphics card of Snapdragon phones (the model started but the test got no sensible answer). Whoever downloaded the old file should delete it and download the model again.
* DeepSeek R1 Distill Qwen 1.5B uses the more exact Q8_0 file (about 1.8 GB), because the smallest model copes badly with heavy compression. Whoever downloaded the old file should delete it and download the model again.
* DeepSeek R1 Distill Qwen 1.5B: recommended minimum is 6 GB of RAM.
* DeepSeek R1 Distill Qwen 1.5B on a phone: the NPU and graphics card are tried first with full-precision attention and cache (they gave nonsense before); if that still fails, the model runs on the processor.
* Reporting problems on a phone: the message sent to the group now carries the description you wrote, not the group's name and link.
* AI models: the whole DeepSeek family (R1 Distill 1.5B, 7B, Llama 8B, R1 0528 Qwen3 8B, 14B, 32B and Llama 70B) has its own tab instead of one model under "Other"; all models are available on both platforms, with a description and memory needs.
* Reporting problems on a computer: the package is saved straight into Downloads (not deep inside the app folders), and after sending, the folder opens with the file already selected, together with the group in the browser, so the file only has to be dragged or attached.
* Assistant: the "i" window now scrolls, so the "Run the compatibility test" button at its bottom is always reachable; the test is described in the window text.
* About screen, "Feature credits": the list now names all the projects the fork's features use (Sniffer, translator, assistant and dictation, Mesh Link Planner, terrain panorama).
* Terrain panorama: an "Only favourites" switch in "Options" shows only favourite nodes in the panorama and on the move map (remembered).
* Terrain panorama: accuracy circles for nodes that do not report an exact position. On the small move map they are ground circles as on the main map, and in the panorama itself an oval the size of the doubt as seen from the viewpoint. Switch them off in "Options" ("Accuracy circles" switch, remembered).
* Terrain panorama: the range, antenna heights and radio-horizon factor are remembered (it no longer returns to 15 km). A new "Nodes up to" option (25–1000 km, 100 km by default) works separately from the terrain range: nodes farther than the loaded terrain are drawn too.
* Terrain panorama: an info icon in the top bar (next to the gear) opens a detailed description of the view, its buttons and options.
* Terrain panorama: nodes in the panorama itself are drawn exactly as on the main map (a dot in the node's colour with a white ring, above it the badge with the short name and a tail, same sizes); the green and red outline stays as a thin frame on the badge.
* Terrain panorama: "Options" moved to the top bar as a gear icon (next to the camera), so the row of buttons below it no longer wraps.
* Terrain panorama, Move: shows a real map like the main one (basemap, panning, zoom, nodes as the same badges with a dot) with a cross in the middle; on letting go the panorama is redrawn from the point under the cross (without the MapLibre map the simple map stays).
* Desktop: a node's details (with the map) are no longer rebuilt when the number of columns changes, so opening an extra screen no longer hesitates; the list and details on desktop now use our own layout with a slider.
* Desktop: the same screen shown twice for a moment (when an extra screen such as neighbours opens) no longer crashes the app; the second copy gets its own saved-state key.
* Desktop: in a narrower window (600 dp and up), when an extra screen (e.g. neighbours) is open over a node's details, the node list slides away and the details and the extra screen share the window (two columns with a slider); three columns only from 1400 dp width (e.g. full screen). The neighbour list no longer counts the placeholder with number 0.
* Desktop: a navigation stack saved from an earlier session with repeated screens (the same node twice) no longer crashes the app at startup; the repeats are removed.
* Desktop: a node opened from the map or the mesh view no longer leaves the same node's details twice in the Nodes tab (after a second tap and opening the neighbour info list the app reported a key used several times).
* Terrain panorama, Offline terrain: a button in Options saves on the device the terrain around the place looked from; you pick how much to save (2, 5, 15, 25, 40, 80 or 100 km, 100 by default; a 100 km range was added); the saved terrain works without the internet in the panorama and in the link planner, its size is shown and it can be deleted.
* Desktop: the mesh view (terrain panorama) opened from a link no longer closes the app, and the mesh view button next to the planner on the map is on the desktop too and works; a tapped node opens in the Nodes tab.
* About: a new "What is MT_SW?" text (app and community) and a hardware text without references to the original's partners.
* Terrain panorama: it turns with the phone (Compass button; touching the view switches the compass off), the Move button shows a map from above that you drag to look from any place; colours from the app theme, chosen buttons with a yellow outline, nodes as coloured badges with the short name as on the map (green/red outline = in sight/hidden).
* Node list, neighbours window: the same look as "Neighbor info" in the log (name, short name, coloured SNR, Cancel/OK buttons).
* Node list, neighbours icon: the number is always the number of neighbours in the last neighbour packet (for our own node too); before, our own node counted all nodes heard directly, up to 15. The icon is now a button: a tap opens a small window with the last neighbour packet received from that node (the neighbour's name and SNR, the time of the packet), without going to the node details. Pictures via a link are no longer made smaller in any way, they go as they are.
* Pictures via a link: fixed the sending stopping half way (it was tied to the conversation screen and had no time limits of its own), the server's error is no longer sent as a message, and a failed sending is visible. Added a progress bar with percentages, cancelling, two automatic retries, and a preview that shows loading and can be retried.
* Database: version 68 (it holds the reaction columns with the MQTT origin added in the original and our log indices); older databases move to it safely, step by step. The local statistics widget has the new look of the original, with our extra statistics (memory in use, processor, PSRAM, flash) and our mark.
* The welcome screen "What MT_SW adds": the remaining features added (AI assistant and translator, terrain panorama, offline knowledge base, reports and logs, adaptive notifications, the node list your way, app updates). The AI settings say that this is an experimental feature, ask for testing of the models and have a "Report notes about models" button. The translator proposes (marked "recommended") the same Gemma models as the assistant.
* MT_SW features: a new "Network planning" section with Mesh Link Planer (opens the planner for your node), the Terrain panorama and the Antenna calculator (before, a separate "Tools" section); both are also in the settings search.
* Mesh view: it now opens first as the **terrain panorama** (the skyline all round with the nodes placed against it: green ones stand above the ridge in front of them, red ones are hidden; range 2/5/15/40 km, antenna height, turned by dragging), and the camera view is an addition under the camera icon. The mesh view button moved from the map's top bar to the map's buttons, next to the planner. The place to look from: your node by default (with its height), or the phone, another node or a typed-in position; antenna height from the node or by hand; range up to 80 km; options: the antenna height of the nodes shown, the radio-horizon factor, the field of view, only nodes in sight, names. The panorama works on the whole map and for a single node. The method is from the Horizon view of MeshRF (GPL-3.0, Ixitxachitl), the terrain from the same source as the planner.
* App version: shown in the settings and in the header of the logs the way the release is named on GitHub (e.g. `v4(2.8.4).test.1` or `v4(2.8.4)`), not as a number with the build code. The release and test numbers come from one place in the code (`MtswRelease`), which is changed together with the release.
* Assistant, fixes after reported errors in a conversation: a node name spoken in several words ("unr base") is recognised as SP7UNR_base instead of pointing at another, similar-sounding node; a polite request ("can you change the telemetry interval…") opens the right setting with a button instead of only refusing; in the `/sprzęt` report the layer count is in Polish ("36 z 36 warstw na NPU").
* Assistant, fixes after the test of the small models (Qwen 3.5 0.8B, Bielik 1.5B): a very small model no longer makes up answers to general questions (recipes, literature, politics) and says it is too small for them, suggesting a bigger model; questions about the band, frequency, power and licence go to the knowledge base instead of the model's "own words" (which gave wrong rules); a model that fails to return the sorting of the question three times in a row is not asked at every question (the word rules decide, retry every 25 questions), which shortens the answer time; the translator removes bold marks round the translation and asks the model once more when it returned the text untranslated; a new knowledge-base entry about a duplicated public key.
* Assistant, fixes after the test of the Gemma 4 models (E2B, E4B, 12B): (1) a question that is word for word the title of a knowledge-base entry gets that entry at once, without an extra run of the model, so the answer is instant and the same in every model; (2) the clock in the instructions for the model moved to the end (it changed every second and made the model read everything after it again at each question); (3) a model that writes slowly (below about 3 words a second, e.g. 12B on a Mac) gets a smaller portion of knowledge and answers more briefly; (4) the "Source" line is no longer attached to general questions (e.g. the capital of France), and a "related" source needs a better match; (5) new knowledge-base entries: sharing a channel, backup and restore of the configuration, going back to the original firmware, run time of an 18650 battery.
* Assistant on a phone: when a model does not fit into the free memory, the app skips the NPU and the graphics card by itself (they copy the whole model into memory) and runs it on the processor, which reads the file in pieces and does not get the app ended by the system; with extremely little memory it shows a message instead of closing. The dictation model and the assistant model no longer sit in memory together when there is no room (one is freed first), and after a failed try (e.g. the NPU) the model is fully released and the app waits (up to 20 s) until the memory is back at its earlier level before trying the next way (e.g. the graphics card). If the system still ends the app while a model loads, that way is skipped for a week. Older Snapdragons (e.g. the 8 Gen 2) have only one NPU session, so a bigger model is tried on a single session at once.
* Reporting bugs and ideas: moved to the MT_SW features. The app keeps the logs of the last 24 hours by itself; the report window lets you pick Discord, WhatsApp or Signal and "Send" builds a package with the logs (phone: share to the chosen app with the file and the group link; computer: save the zip, open its folder and the group).
* Assistant: after the first model download it asks for consent to a compatibility test (a short question, the `/sprzęt` report, a dictation check with the smallest model); the result goes into one package in the report window, nothing is sent by itself. The test can be repeated from the "i" window.
* Assistant: the header of the chat (the notice and the copy/save buttons) folds away when the chat is scrolled with a finger and comes back on a swipe down, so it does not cover the chat on a strongly enlarged screen; the switch is in the info window.
* A current inverted with the polarity switch is now shown with the right sign also on the node list (the extra line), in the node details (environment sensors and power channels) and in the assistant's answers. After the migration they showed the sensor's own sign.
* The firmware version is shown short (for example 2.8.2), without the build number and additions, in the node details (installed, latest stable and alpha), on the connection card and in the OnDemand statistics.

#### October 2026
* AI settings: the "Skip NPU" switch and the button that clears failed attempts are shown at once only on computers with an NPU the engine can use (Intel Core Ultra); on phones and on the other computers they are hidden.
* Intel NPU: Every model tries the Intel NPU (Gemma E4B is the checked one there), and a model that fails is tried again after a week instead of 3 days (on a computer at once after an NPU driver update). The same goes for the NPU of a phone. A start on the NPU can take up to 20 minutes, because the model is compiled every time (saving the compiled model on disk causes an error on this NPU, so it is not used). The `/sprzet` report shows how long the start took and, on an error, the last lines of the program's log.
* AI assistant: the engine writes more about the chips into the log (graphics card names and their memory, the split of the model, layers), so `/sprzet` shows which card the model really runs on. When a computer has no microphone, the log lists the sound devices the system sees.
* AI assistant and dictation on Android 17: the engine programs (Hexagon NPU, graphics card) start again. Android 17 used to reject them at start (an older library from the maker's folder was found before the system's one), so the model ran only on the processor and very slowly.
* AI assistant: when a chip (NPU, graphics card) does not start the model, the `/sprzet` report gives the concrete reason (the program's exit code and the last error line of its log) instead of the general "the chip did not work".
* AI assistant: the `/sprzet` command also shows where the model ran last time when it was released from memory after being idle (also after the app restarts), and how much memory it takes now. The report also shows how the model memory is split between chips (NPU, graphics card, processor), the layers on the card or NPU, the chat memory and the chips found; it is in Polish.
* AI models: family names have no version numbers (Gemma, Qwen, Bielik...), because newer models will arrive in the families. The biggest Qwen, Qwen 3.8 27B, joined the Qwen family. A new "Other" tab holds curiosities for your own tests: PLLuM, gpt-oss 20B from OpenAI, Granite 4.2 8B from IBM, Phi-4 mini, DeepSeek R1 Qwen3 8B, Mistral Small 3.2 24B, Llama 3.2 1B and OLMoE 1B-7B (all but gpt-oss in the same Q4_0 format, so they can be compared; all shown on computers and phones; the Snapdragon NPU has a limit of about 7 GB); a note about this is shown at the top of the tab. Qwen 3.5 35B A3B was replaced by the newer Qwen 3.6 35B A3B; when a model is replaced by a newer one, the app removes its old files at start.
* Assistant: the quote mode picks a knowledge-base entry by its text as well as its title (for example "what hop limit is recommended" finds the entry "How many hops to set?"); before, such questions ended with "I have no entry".
* Assistant: questions about the app's own screens and settings (interface, font, theme, chat) no longer go to the internet (the results were about Windows and phones), and questions about the mesh and the radio are searched with "Meshtastic" added.
* Assistant: sixth batch of fixes. The internet search returned nothing (DuckDuckGo rejected the program's requests), so even "capital of France" ended with "I don't know": the request now goes with a browser header, and when that is not enough the assistant asks Wikipedia (Polish, then English) and quotes the fitting sentences. The smallest models no longer offer a "closest entry" that did not fit the question, and a single word of a question is enough for a quote only for short technical terms (SNR, LNA, SAW). For settings questions a small model first gets the lines of the app manual about the found setting.
* Assistant: fifth batch of fixes after another Qwen 0.8B test. The app no longer quotes "no results" notices or connection errors as internet answers (this used to produce "The web results do not answer this question clearly"); when the internet returns nothing, the small model says honestly that it has no answer. The smallest models no longer retell knowledge-base entries in their own words for app questions (the source of the nonsense about "code updates"): the entry is quoted, or the closest entry with a note that it may not fit directly. Short terms (SNR, GPS, USB, LNA, SAW) and "Wi-Fi" are recognised when entries are matched. "How much battery does node X have" is read by the app from the node database, and "what day is it today" from the clock.
* Assistant: fourth round of fixes after testing Qwen 0.8B. Weaker models (under about 3 GB of RAM) no longer make up answers: when the knowledge base has no fitting entry, the app goes to the internet (if it is on) and quotes the best-fitting sentences of the results, and without internet it honestly says it has no answer; jokes, poems and code are declined with a hint to pick a larger model. Role descriptions (CLIENT, ROUTER_LATE, TRACKER and others) come from the documentation, written by the app. The app also answers itself questions about: your own hop limit, "who wrote to me", the number of messages from a node, the channel named in the question ("on channel MT_SW"), airtime and channel use of your own radio, the number of routers, nodes that disappeared, a network status report and "how many hops do I hear node X from". "Go to node X" opens the node, "check the link between X and my node" opens the planner for X, and requests for settings ("Open display/network/position settings", "transmit power") land in the right place. The text of a prepared message is taken word for word from what you wrote. The calculator counts coaxial cable loss (RG-58, RG-8X, RG-213, LMR-400). The translator retries when the model changes a name, a number with its unit or a link. New knowledge base entries: SF, bandwidth, coding rate, SNR, RSSI, duty cycle, region and connecting over Bluetooth.
* Assistant: third round of fixes after testing Bielik 1.5B, Qwen 0.8B and Gemma. The app itself (not the model's guess) answers questions about: nearest and farthest nodes, nodes heard in a given time, packet statistics, a node's power source and availability, nodes with low battery or GPS, favourites, inactive nodes, your own preset, role, region and channels (read from the radio settings), "where is node X" and "who wrote to me this week". Requests like "set the power/preset" only open the setting (the assistant changes nothing); "can you send a message" and "do you speak English" have fixed answers; a weather-forecast question honestly says there is no forecast and shows the sensor readings. "Which battery is best" and "what is Meshtastic" are no longer counted as a ranking or a network summary. Small models no longer invent steps for "how do I…" questions when the knowledge base has no entry; leaked tool-call JSON is replaced by a short explanation; follow-up questions ("and this week?") inherit the topic. Translator: the model's answer is cleaned of ```json frames and labels, and a too-short result (part of the text) is retried. Thanks ("thank you for the help") get a fixed answer without a source. Every message in the assistant window now shows the full date and time (not just the time).
* Dictation: for short recordings Whisper now uses a shortened audio window (the length of the recording with some room to spare, not below 10 s) instead of always 30 s, which makes dictation faster; an empty or suspicious result is repeated at once with the full window.
* Dictation: the model list starts with the Whisper models, marked as recommended (accurate, fast and light); Gemma 4 with speech support comes after them, as an alternative.
* Translator: the `/tłumacz` command also works in the translator itself and runs the translation test (the same set of sentences for every model, with a check of names, numbers and links and the time); the results can be copied, and on a computer they are saved in a separate file for each model.
* AI assistant on phones with an older graphics driver (no Vulkan 1.2): the start rejects the graphics card at once instead of loading the whole model three times, so the model starts faster and uses less memory.
* Assistant: fixes after tests on four models. The preset table now lists every preset (Lite, Turbo and Tiny too); the transmit power is given by the app (in dBm and mW), not by the model; the uptime of one node, messages "from" a node and "farthest from" work whatever a small model gets wrong; a weak-signal question leads to Mesh Link Planer, not to the presets; a question about the neighbor icon leads to the setting in the node list; the source of a knowledge-base answer is added by the app; a question about "new nodes" honestly says the app does not record when a node first appeared; the assistant says "mesh network", not "MT_SW network". The knowledge base got entries on MT_SW_FIRMWARE, battery, range records, the SAW filter, collecting logs and choosing an AI model.
* Assistant: second round of fixes after tests. Questions about messages ("who wrote to me", "did anyone write about the weather"), the node count, the nodes of a given role and the facts of one node are now answered by the app from the real data instead of the model guessing. Sensitivity, speed and differences between presets are computed from the preset table; the free-space range is described as pure theory and the real radio-horizon range comes first. Roles and presets carry Polish names with the English one in brackets (e.g. "Router pomocniczy (ROUTER_LATE)", "Wąski - szybki (Narrow Fast)"), "traceroute" is called "Wyznaczanie trasy" in the Polish app, and the assistant understands both spellings. The model knows the date and time, and exports and the test report show the full date next to the time. Answers no longer refer to MT_SW needlessly. The internet is only a fallback (when the knowledge base has no answer) and only for the stronger Gemma-family models; weaker models are not given web results. The smallest models quote a knowledge-base entry word for word in English too, and the source line is always written by the app. New knowledge-base entries: the T-Beam radio chip, cable losses (RG-58, LMR-400, RG-213), Polish and English names, saving the translator conversation, and model choice with a warning that outside the Gemma family answers cannot be guaranteed 100%.
* Assistant: the answer to "what can you do" is fixed and the same on every model; when it does not know an answer it suggests that question. Questions about a setting the manual does not describe (for example saving logs, the neighbor icon) get a description from the knowledge base with the button under it. Fixed the removal of wrong "Source" lines written by the model (capital Ź) and the node uptime for every kind of question.
* Assistant: quote mode for the smallest models (under about 3 GB RAM, e.g. Qwen 0.8B, Bielik 1.5B): when the knowledge base has a clearly matching entry, the assistant gives it word for word with a “Source” line instead of letting a weak model garble steps and numbers. Stronger models answer as before.
* Assistant: after tests on three models — quote mode picks the entry by its title and does not quote tables or “related” entries; the “Source” line only appears when the entry fits the question; the model knows the current date and time; node data (hardware, firmware, last heard, distance) and simple spoken arithmetic (“divided by”, “squared”) are answered by the app; the “weak range” template only for questions about the own radio; saved conversations and reports show the full date next to the time; a new entry on T-Beam radio chips; a note on the recommended model family.
* Translator: the translations of the current session collect on the translator screen and can be saved as a file (PDF, Markdown, TXT, HTML, JSON) with “Save translations…”; “Clear” deletes them.
* App data folder: in the "MT_SW features" (a row for everyone, on every system) you can choose where the app keeps its data. On a computer (Windows, Mac, Linux) it is one folder for the message database, the settings, the logs, the maps and the AI models and programs; the app asks for the place on the first start, the move is made at the next start (a small window with the progress, the files are checked after copying and the old ones removed only then), and before a model download that would leave less than 50 GB free the app asks for confirmation. On a phone you can choose the place of the AI models (the phone's memory or an SD card).
* Dictation: a chip (NPU, graphics card) that writes nonsense or is slower than the processor is rejected. The test on silence now checks the result and the speed, and nonsense during dictation switches the chip off and dictation moves to the next way at once. Added a build with the i8mm instructions for the phone's processor (about 10% faster), Vulkan in a safe mode and OpenCL without the Adreno kernels; the Whisper model list has only the Q8 versions (small, medium, large v3 turbo), the same on every system.
* Intel NPU (Windows): first a try with a window of 8192 and the --swa-full option, then with a window of 1024; the assistant's window is matched to what works. The /sprzet report in the assistant is in Polish and without raw logs.
* AI assistant: the questions "How do I set up a channel?" and "How can I improve antenna range?" go to the advice from the knowledge base, not to a search of messages or a check of your radio.
* Dictation on phones: Whisper now tries the NPU (Hexagon, Snapdragon 8 Gen 2 and newer), then the graphics card (OpenCL or Vulkan), and last the processor; a chip that does not work is skipped for a few days, and before a chip is accepted the app does a test run with a second of silence. New Whisper models in the Q8 format (small, medium, large v3 turbo), because the NPU only does Q8. The programs for the chips are built by the workflow "Build llama.cpp and whisper.cpp for Android".
* Dictation on computers: Whisper now uses the graphics card (Metal on a Mac with Apple Silicon, Vulkan on Windows and Linux) and falls back to the processor when it does not work or fails the test run (a Mac now has its own dictation program; it had none before). The Whisper model list is the same on every system. The programs are built by the workflow "Build whisper.cpp for computers".
* AI assistant: asked which preset to use, it now shows a table of the presets (speed, sensitivity, packet time, about how many devices fit) worked out from the presets' parameters, for the chosen error correction and the number of devices given; in our network the preset is fixed and the assistant says not to change it.
* AI assistant: fixes to answers worked out in code — "which node is farthest from X" counts from X (not from your radio), "do I have range to X" answers from what the radio heard (directly or through other nodes, last signal, distance), "which nodes are routers" lists the nodes with that role, a node's RSSI and SNR always come from the app's data, and the time written in the question ("24 hours") wins over the model's wrong guess. Greetings, "how are you" and thanks get a fixed reply.
* AI assistant: an answer that was cut by the length limit now ends at the last whole sentence (or with an ellipsis), a repeated word is detected and stopped, models get a light penalty for repeating, and web addresses a model made up without the internet are removed from the answer.
* AI assistant: answers in the tests (/szybki, /test) now carry the answer time, the model and the route like a normal conversation, also in the JSON export.
* Dictation on phones: the NPU (Hexagon) libraries are added to the signal-processor path, the graphics cards (OpenCL, Vulkan) run without flash attention (it crashed OpenCL and broke the Adreno driver in Vulkan), and the remembered failed attempts of the chips are cleared once so they get a new try.
* Dictation: faster on all platforms. Dictation for the chat now knows the language (the app language), so Whisper skips the extra pass that guesses the language; dictation in the translator still detects the language itself. Decoding without repeated attempts and without beam search. The log records the dictation time and the length of the recording.
* AI assistant: a question about the LoRa region ("Which region should I set?", "How do I change the region?") gets a description of what the region is for and a hint fitted to where you are: from the radio's position, or when there is none, from the phone's location (on phones, only at that moment, nothing is sent or stored). In Poland and Europe it is EU_868, and in the Świętokrzyskie voivodeship our EU_N_868 with the Narrow Fast preset (869.44165 MHz). A button to the setting is shown under the answer.
* Assistant knowledge base: a new entry on updating firmware for each chip separately (nRF52, ESP32, RP2040, STM32): OTA over Bluetooth or Wi-Fi, DFU mode, UF2 file, USB, Web Flasher, SWD programmer, and what to do when OTA does not work.
* Assistant knowledge base: hop limit — from firmware 2.8.x we recommend 7 (variable, lowered automatically for less important packets; messages keep the full limit), 3 for older firmware; a new entry "How to report a bug or idea": MT_SW bugs go to us (Discord, groups, the mesh), bugs of the original app to its GitHub. The documentation (Debug panel, translations) says the same.
* AI assistant: the JSON save of a conversation carries more data for diagnosis with every answer: the model, how the question was sorted, the number of words and characters, the speed (words per second), whether the internet was used, whether the answer only points to a setting and whether it may have been cut off.
* AI assistant: every message in the chat shows a small grey time: the time sent under a question, the time received and the answer time in seconds under an answer (for example "12:03:17 · 12.3 s"), also in saved conversations.
* AI assistant: the sorting of questions is now checked by code, not only by the small model. Questions like "Who wrote Pan Tadeusz?", "What is the LoRa range record?" or "How do I reduce battery use?" no longer end up in the message search, the radio statistics or the battery reading, but in the knowledge base and the model. "Which nodes are routers?" shows the list of routers. The assistant always introduces itself as the assistant of the MT_SW app (never of the original app), in the language of the question. Copied instructions and empty "Source: None" lines disappear from answers, and sources are given only for the fragments the model really used.
* AI assistant: answers are faster and more to the point. Quiet thinking is off (on Gemma, Bielik and PLLuM it cost tens of seconds on every question), answers have a length limit and a request to be brief, and the instruction for the model is shorter. The internet is searched only when it can help (not for greetings and questions about the assistant, not for very short questions, not when the knowledge base already answers; fresh facts such as prices, laws and dates always). Knowledge questions ("what happens if...", "which preset...", "how do I do a range test") are answered by the model from the knowledge base and get the button to a setting only as an extra (before, many of them ended with just a pointer to a setting or a pasted description of the project). The question about the set transmit power shows the settings, not the radio statistics. Greetings go straight to the answer without a separate step of understanding the question. A request to "forget the rules" is politely refused.
* AI assistant: saved conversations (JSON, text, Markdown) carry the time each message was sent or received, and for answers also how long they took. The tests (`/test`, `/szybki` ...) show for every question the times, the answer time, the number of words and whether the internet was used, and a summary at the end: model, number of questions, average, median and longest time, number of empty answers and answers that only point to a setting, number of internet uses, average answer length and words per second.
* AI assistant: new light models: Qwen 3.5 0.8B (about 0.5 GB, the lightest) and Bielik 1.5B v3 (about 1.7 GB, the smallest Polish one).
* AI assistant: the Qwen 3.5 models (2B, 4B, 9B) use the Q4_0 version instead of Q4_K_M, on phones and computers. With Q4_K_M the graphics card of Adreno phones wrote nonsense and Vulkan could not build its shaders; the engines (GPU and NPU) handle Q4_0 best. The models have to be downloaded again, and the replaced old model files are removed by the app at start (only older versions of the same model, in its own folder).
* AI assistant on phones: failed graphics cards are now remembered per model (a card may run one model and crash on another), and Vulkan gets a second try in safe mode with the newer driver features switched off (some Adreno phones cannot build the fast shaders). The `/sprzet` report shows the reason when a chip crashes.
* AI assistant on phones: after starting on the NPU or the graphics card the app asks the model a short test question; if the chip crashes or answers with nonsense (OpenCL does that on some Adreno chips), that chip is not used for this model and the app moves on to the next one.
* AI assistant on phones: the app wrongly decided that the graphics card and the NPU "did not take part", because the new engine no longer prints the line with the layer count, and remembered those chips as broken (the model ran on the processor alone and answered very slowly). Chips are now also recognised by other log entries, and the remembered wrong marks are cleared once. The NPU setting uses its new name (the old one was deprecated).
* Settings: the assistant, the translator and dictation are now on their own "Smart features" screen (MT_SW features holds just one row with a short description, so it is no longer so long). At the top are the switches of the functions, and at the bottom the model choice in tabs.
* AI assistant: the `/sprzet` command also shows a device description (phone, chip, memory, graphics driver) for collecting compatibility reports; remembered failed chip attempts are cleared once so the NPU and the graphics card get a fresh chance. Dictation has its own switch (on by default), and chosen models are no longer overwritten at start. The neighbours icon on the node list now sits in the time row, centred between the time and the icons on the right.
* AI assistant: NPU support — on phones with a Snapdragon 8 Gen 2 or newer (Hexagon) and on computers with an Intel Core Ultra (OpenVINO), and the Adreno graphics on Windows with a Snapdragon X; the app picks the chip by itself and moves to the graphics card when the NPU cannot cope with a model. On a computer the graphics card is now filled with the model almost to the top (before, the engine left 1 GB free, so a 4 GB card used only about 2 GB), and only the rest goes to ordinary memory. In the settings, the "Free AI memory" item has its title above the description (before, the title fell apart into a vertical column of letters).
* AI assistant: the `/sprzet` command now also shows how the last model start went (each attempt: NPU, graphics card, processor — whether it worked and, if not, why); a chip that does not answer within 4 minutes is dropped. Fixed the doubled percent sign while a model downloads ("93%%"). The Snapdragon NPU now gets the model layers to work on (before, the chip was detected but the model stayed on the processor).
* AI assistant: the model stays in memory while you use it (it no longer switches off after 2 minutes without questions), and when another model is chosen the old one is switched off at once and the new one is loaded right away; switching a function off frees its model, a model left behind by a force-closed or frozen app is switched off at the next start (now on macOS and Windows too), and the settings have a "Free AI memory" item that shows the model in memory and switches it off right away. Qwen models answer without quiet thinking (before, it made answers take many minutes), and for the other models thinking that goes on too long is cut short. Faster answers on a computer: a bigger engine cache, so the long instructions for the model are not read again from the start for every question, and during a test (`/test`, `/szybki` …) the assistant takes no notes from the test questions (before, every test question ran the model once more and ended up in the assistant's memory). Calculator: "link budget for X km" works out the margin at that distance (before, it showed antenna dimensions), "Open the knowledge base" opens the knowledge base (before, the node database cleanup), and the translator test accepts a number with a comma or a dot (8,6 = 8.6).
* Phone: the same choice of models as on the computer (assistant, translator) and Whisper for dictation; models that do not fit in a phone's memory show their requirements.
* Desktop (macOS): the save and open file windows are in Polish (the app declares the Polish language to the system, and the window titles follow the language).
* AI assistant and translator: moved to one new engine (llama.cpp) on phone and computer, and the old Google LiteRT-LM engine is removed completely. Settings now have Assistant / Translator / Dictation tabs with the models grouped in families (Gemma, Qwen, Bielik, TranslateGemma, Whisper), with a description, licence, model page and a "recommended" / "alternative" mark. Nothing downloads by itself: after a function is switched on, a red text "Choose a model to install" appears under the switch. The old models and cache of the previous engine are removed once at the first start. Dictation has its own model choice (Whisper on a computer, Gemma E2B/E4B). The assistant has an "i" icon with a description and new test commands: `/narzedzia`, `/rozmowa`, `/baza`, `/tlumacz`, `/sprzet` (`/test` runs everything, translations too, about 450 questions). On phones the app first tries its own engine builds with the graphics chip (OpenCL on a Snapdragon, then Vulkan on any phone), and uses the processor build when the chip takes no part; the choice is automatic. The neighbour icon in the node list stands in the same column on every card, just before the icons on the right.

* Assistant: general knowledge base, also beyond Meshtastic (electronics, radio and wave propagation, IT, 3D printing, work at height, shopping and customs, everyday life, first aid, hiking, devices including DIY ones; on network topics: antennas, filters and LNAs, radio roles and settings, LoRa presets, channels and encryption, MQTT, modules, flashing and updating firmware, building, `userPrefs`, power and solar, building your own devices, Home Assistant, why a LoRa network cannot be too large). Entries are plain text files in `knowledge/base`, each with a source and the date it was checked; the assistant thinks about the entries it finds and answers in its own words, and says it does not know when the base lacks something. With the internet switch on, web results are shown apart and compared with the base.
* AI assistant on the computer: the app's memory limit is now half of the computer's memory instead of a fixed 2 GB, and the assistant's log records how much memory the model start takes, how long it takes and how fast the answer is written (characters per second), so it is visible whether the work runs on the graphics card.
* Logs: a switch for saving logs in MT_SW features (off by default, the choice is kept), once on, the app saves its whole life on its own (no buttons), a log in parts of about 400 KB with headers and a table of contents, on a phone a copy in Downloads/MT_SW_logi that refreshes itself, and the cause of a crash always saved separately (no message texts, keys or positions).
* Assistant: the knowledge base reaches the answers again (it was never passed to the model), "what is…" questions check the base first, and the graphics chip is tried on every model load with a full account in the log.
* Knowledge base: a new "Knowledge base" item in MT_SW Features for browsing the whole base offline (search, three tiers, expandable entries with sources); the assistant uses it too.
* Node list: a people icon with a digit (the neighbour count from the last saved list, on our own node the nodes heard directly) in the name line, in the middle of the free space between the name and the icons on the right; it only informs and can be switched off in the node list layout.
* Merge with the original: local node display names (list, details, messages, maps, widget), traceroutes other nodes ran to us on our own node and a new database version 67 (ours, because the original took 66); nodes at one spot are still spread in a circle, without the counted grouping.
* AI assistant: a newer version of the model library (0.18.1, the same as in Google's own tool, where this model runs on the graphics card) and three attempts to start on the graphics card (as is, after clearing the cache, with a smaller window); each attempt is in the log.
* The assistant counts by itself, without guessing: "which node has the lowest voltage / the longest uptime / the highest CPU load…", averages and a quick summary of the nodes for over 20 values (voltage, battery, uptime, channel, CPU, RAM, flash, PSRAM, Wi-Fi, traffic, signal, hops, distance, temperature and more). A new value is one row in a catalogue, not new handling.
* Assistant: it thinks longer before answering (a larger quiet-thinking budget, the same on a phone and a computer), understands a request for the list of nodes (shows them, not just the count) and questions about the highest and lowest voltage, CPU load, RAM, flash, PSRAM and Wi-Fi signal of the nodes. In the computer report the graphics card shows its real memory (Windows reported 4 GB at most).
* **Adaptive notifications** (formerly Live Update, Android 16+): new name and moved from the connections screen to Settings, next to notification management. Mesh status now shows everything about your node (uptime, channel load, traffic, noise, CPU, RAM, flash, PSRAM, Wi-Fi — as percentages), and node tracking also shows the chosen node's role, channel load, traffic and hardware load.
* Desktop: the app no longer crashes when the window is minimised, hidden or being restored (window size error); the previous layout is kept until the size is valid again.
* "Heap" is now called **RAM** across the whole app, and used memory (RAM, flash, PSRAM) is shown as a percentage everywhere too (node list, notification, widget, sniffer, on-demand diagnostics). The note for extended extra telemetry now also mentions the Wi-Fi signal strength.
* Desktop: with three columns (list, details and an extra screen, for example on-demand diagnostics) there are now two sliders for changing the width; before, three columns had none. The widths last until the app is closed.
* **Extra telemetry** (MT_SW_FIRMWARE v3): a “Local statistics” card in the telemetry settings with switches for extra and extended extra telemetry and a separate interval for each (2 hours at least). Both options are saved together; the app first reads the state from the node and shows what the node stored. It works from MT_SW_FIRMWARE v3; on an older one the card is visible but nothing in it reacts, with a note. A shortcut is also in MT_SW features.
* **The assistant rebuilt from scratch.** The model first recognises what you ask in a fixed format (it can only pick one of the known kinds, so it no longer breaks tool calls), then: exact answers (counts, searches, node lists, routes, status, sensors) are written by the app's code in the app's language, questions about the app are answered by the model from the documentation, questions about the network from data chosen for them, and any other topic can be talked about freely. Short instructions for the model in the language of the conversation, quiet thinking before the answer and smoother display of the answer.
* The assistant **goes there by itself** when you ask: a node on the map (also the nearest or farthest one, for example the nearest router), a screen, a setting (it blinks yellow), the link planner, a conversation with the message text ready. It never changes a setting and never sends a message.
* The translator and the assistant are marked as an **experimental feature**: correct operation is not guaranteed.
* Assistant: a permanent **`/test`** command — it asks the test questions by itself and gives a report to copy (on a computer also a file), to compare answers across devices and models. The network summary and antenna lengths are worked out by the app.
* Assistant: recognises node names in another form (e.g. “Karczówce” for “Karczowka”), suggests similar names when it does not know a node, and remembers the context of the conversation: the whole conversation (older exchanges are summarised as it goes, the newest stay word for word) and the node talked about last (e.g. just giving the node name, or “and what is the battery there?”); with memory on it also knows the notes about you (e.g. which node is yours at home) and saves them when you tell something about yourself. The desktop version writes logs and crashes to the file `.meshtastic/logs/mt_sw_desktop.log`.
* Assistant: **"Copy whole conversation"** and **"Save conversation…"** buttons above the chat — copy or save all questions and answers (with the redirect buttons) as a PDF (looks like the chat), Markdown, TXT, HTML or JSON, e.g. to send from a phone when testing. JSON was also added to the link planner export and to saving logs in the debug panel (packets, device logs, logcat).
* Assistant: **calculations are done by the app, not the model** — antenna lengths (quarter-wave, dipole, 5/8, Yagi, with build steps) for any frequency, wavelength, dBm↔mW/W, EIRP/ERP with the limit of our band, path loss, Fresnel zone, radio horizon, theoretical range from the preset and power, packet time on air and plain sums. For "weak range" the assistant checks your radio's settings and reception. Numbers in answers from the internet are checked against the source; when they do not match, the assistant quotes the source.
* **Antenna calculator** (MT_SW features → Tools): sizes of 13 antenna types (ground plane, dipole, folded dipole, 5/8, J-pole, Slim Jim, coaxial collinear, Yagi 3–20 el., Moxon, Biquad, helix, patch, coax sections) for any frequency, taking wire thickness and insulation into account, with a drawing and the values of each part; the feed line (transmit power taken from your radio or the band limit) with cable (loss for the frequency), connectors and SWR: losses, power at the antenna, EIRP/ERP, the band limit and the effect on range. The feed line is worked out like in the Mesh Link Planner (any number of connectors and cable sections from the cable database); **My antennas** save an antenna with its feed line, power and gain, and the planner can pick them or open the calculator; the **“Use in the planner”** button (point A or B) opens the planner with that antenna, only when you tap it. The **“Save result…”** button saves the input and results as PDF (a text report without the drawing), Markdown, TXT, CSV (dimension table) or JSON. The assistant links to it.
* Dictation: a **"Headset microphone for dictation"** switch — with a headset connected (Bluetooth, wired, USB) dictation everywhere in the app records from its microphone.
* **One AI model** for the assistant, the translator and dictation, chosen in one place (choosing NLLB, which only translates, turns the assistant off after a warning and confirmation).
* MT_SW features: a shortcut to **picking the node colour** (the button in the security settings blinks); the button is named in the app's language.
* Black-and-white themes: node markers on the map (the name bubbles) and the buttons over the map (the compass with its red arrow, zoom) are grey too.
* The assistant knows our network's settings (Narrow Fast preset, EU_N_868 region, 869.44165 MHz), answers two questions asked in one, works out antenna lengths from the frequency.
* Assistant: with the internet switched on the app runs the web search itself (when the question is about facts to look up), and a small note in the app's colour under the answer shows what was searched and the sources. The `/test` command in the assistant asks the test questions by itself and saves the results.
